First, one can observe that
\varkappa^{\alpha}_{p}
is defined such that

|\kappa^{\alpha}_{p}(x)|\leq a^{\alpha}_{p}|x|+b^{\alpha}_{p}=\varkappa^{\alpha}_{p}(|x|),\quad\forall x\in\mathds{R},

and for
x=\varnothing
,
\kappa^{\alpha}_{p}(x)=\varkappa^{\alpha}_{p}(x)=0
.

Thus if
x
and
y
are such that

x=\varnothing\Rightarrow y=\varnothing,\quad\text{and}\quad x\in\mathds{R}\Rightarrow y\in\mathds{R},\ |x|\leq y

then we have
|\kappa^{\alpha}_{p}(x)|\leq\varkappa^{\alpha}_{p}(y)
for any
\alpha\in\bm{M}\cup\bm{P}
and
p\in\bm{P}
.

Point 1.
That fact that assumption
\bm{(}\text{{{NoExpl}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}^{+}]
holds is clear since it holds for
\mathfrak{M}
. It is also clear that the initial condition
\mathfrak{C}^{+}
is integrable since
\mathfrak{C}
is integrable.

Let
t_{0}=T_{0}<T_{1}<T_{2}<\cdots<T_{n}<\cdots
be the ordered random sequence of both the points of
(S^{m}\cap[t_{0},\infty))_{\in\bm{M}}
of the
\HAR_{\mathfrak{M}}(t_{0},\mathfrak{C},\xi)
process and the domains of the chains:
\cup_{p\in\bm{P}}\mathcal{D}_{p}\cap[t_{0},\infty)
. We will prove that the result holds for all
t\in[T_{0},T_{n}]
, for all
C\subset[T_{0},T_{n}]
, and all
(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}
with
T_{0}\leq k\leq T_{n}
by induction on
n
.

n=0
:
It is clear that the result holds by construction of
\mathfrak{M}^{+}
,
\mathfrak{C}^{+}
and
\zeta
.

n\to n+1
:
Let us first remark that by induction and by construction of
\mathfrak{C}^{+}
we have, for all
\alpha\in\bm{M}\cup\bm{P}
and all
(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}
with
k\leq T_{n}
(which is the same as
k<T_{n+1}
),

|\kappa^{\alpha}_{p}(W^{p}_{k})|\leq\varkappa^{\alpha}_{p}((W^{p}_{k})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}).

(10.1)

Since for
\mathfrak{M}
there is no chain nor point in
(T_{n},T_{n+1})
, we have, for
t\in(T_{n},T_{n+1}]
,

\ell^{m}_{t}=\mu_{m}+\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{n}}h^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(t-s)dS^{m^{\prime}}_{s}+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{n}}h^{m}_{p}(t-k)\kappa^{m}_{p}(W^{p}_{k})d{D}^{p}_{k}.

By definition one has,

\displaystyle(\lambda^{m}_{t})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}=\Phi^{m}(\mu_{m})+\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t-}L_{m}|h^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(t-s)|d(S^{m^{\prime}}_{s})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t-}L_{m}|h^{m}_{p}(t-k)|\varkappa^{m}_{p}((W^{p}_{k})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}})d{D}^{p}_{k}

But since by induction we have every
\mathfrak{M}
-point before
T_{n}
is also a
\mathfrak{M}^{+}
-point and that each possible
\mathfrak{M}^{+}
-point in
(T_{n},t)
has a non negative contribution to
(\lambda^{m}_{t})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}
, we have,

\displaystyle(\lambda^{m}_{t})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}\geq\Phi^{m}(\mu_{m})+\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{n}}L_{m}|h^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(t-s)|dS^{m^{\prime}}_{s}

\displaystyle+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{n}}L_{m}|h^{m}_{p}(t-k)||\kappa^{m}_{p}(W^{p}_{k})|d{D}^{p}_{k}.

Let us check that
L_{m}|\ell^{m}_{t}-\mu_{m}|\leq(\lambda^{m}_{t})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}-\Phi^{m}(\mu_{m})
. First,

\left|\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{n}}h^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(t-s)dS^{m^{\prime}}_{s}\right|\leq\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{n}}|h^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(t-s)|dS^{m^{\prime}}_{s}.

(10.2)

Secondly,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{n}}h^{m}_{p}(t-k)\kappa^{m}_{p}(W^{p}_{k})d{D}^{p}_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{n}}|h^{m}_{p}(t-k)||\kappa^{m}_{p}(W^{p}_{k})|d{D}^{p}_{k}.

Combining this inequality with (
10.2
) and the lower bound on
(\lambda^{m}_{t})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}
we obtain the result.
Then, since
\Phi^{m}
is
L_{m}
Lipschitz, we have

\lambda^{m}_{t}=\Phi^{m}(\ell^{m}_{t})\leq\Phi^{m}(\mu_{m})+L_{m}|\ell^{m}_{t}-\mu_{m}|\leq(\lambda^{m}_{t})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}.

Therefore in
(T_{n},T_{n+1}]
every
\mathfrak{M}
-point is also an
\mathfrak{M}^{+}
-point. Finally, if there exists
p\in\bm{P}
such that
(p,T_{n+1})\in\mathcal{Q}
we have

\displaystyle|W^{p}_{T_{n+1}}|

\displaystyle=\left|\xi^{p}_{T_{n+1}}+\Phi^{p}\bigg(\sum_{m}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{n+1}-}h^{p}_{m}(T_{n+1}-s)dS^{m}_{s}+\sum_{p^{\prime}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{n+1}-}h^{p}_{p^{\prime}}(T_{n+1}-k^{\prime})\kappa^{p}_{p^{\prime}}(W^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}})d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}\bigg)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\zeta^{p}_{T_{n+1}}+\sum_{m}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{n+1}-}L_{p}|h^{p}_{m}(T_{n+1}-s)|d(S^{m}_{s})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\sum_{p^{\prime}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{n+1}-}L_{p}|h^{p}_{p^{\prime}}(T_{n+1}-k^{\prime})|\varkappa^{p}_{p^{\prime}}((W^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}})d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(W^{p}_{T_{n+1}})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}},

which concludes the proof of
point 1
.

Point 2.
First let us check that
\mathfrak{M}^{+}
satisfies the assumptions of Theorem
3.9
. The assumptions on the random drifts
\bm{(}\text{{{FiMe}}}\bm{)}
[\zeta]
is clear since
\zeta=|\xi|
. Then an easy check shows that
H_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}=H_{\mathfrak{M}}
thus assumption
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec1}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}^{+}]
is also clear.

Finally one easily checks by induction on
n
, using the exact same ideas of the proof of
point 1
, that the following is true in the Picard iteration from the proof of Theorem
3.9
,

\forall n\in\mathds{N},\ \left\{\begin{aligned} &\forall m\in\bm{M},\ \forall t\in\mathds{R},\ L_{m}|\ell^{m,n}_{t}-\mu_{m}|\leq(\lambda^{m,n}_{t})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}-\Phi^{m}(\mu_{m})\\
&\forall m\in\bm{M},\ \forall t\in\mathds{R},\ \lambda^{m,n}_{t}\leq(\lambda^{m,n}_{t})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}\\
&\forall(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q},\ |W^{p,n}_{k}|\leq(W^{p,n}_{k})_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}\end{aligned}\right..

Since the result is true at each step of the Picard iteration, it is true for the limit, which concludes.
∎

10.2
Proof of Theorem
5.3

The proof of Theorem
5.3
is based on Lemma
C.1
, in Appendix
C
, which derives functional exponential moments for linear combinations of the random drifts and a large class of point processes with independent clusters defined in Section
4.3
and immigrants rates depending linear combinations of the random drifts.

First let us remark that under assumption
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec2}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
, we have
\|h^{p}_{m}\|^{\infty}_{1,1}<\infty
and thus for any
n\in\mathds{N}^{*}
we have, for any
m,p\in\bm{M},\bm{P}
,

\|h^{p}_{m}\|^{\infty}_{1,1/n}\leq\|h^{p}_{m}\|^{\infty}_{1,1}<\infty.

(10.3)

We continue by fitting the immigrant rate to the framework of Lemma
C.1
. From Theorem
4.5
,

\mathcal{I}^{m}_{t}=\mu_{m}+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t-}\left[h^{m}_{p}(t-k)\times(a^{m}_{p}\Hd^{b}_{p,k}+a^{m}_{p}\Hd^{\xi}_{p,k}+b^{m}_{p})\right]d{D}^{p}_{k}

is the immigration part of the intensity at time
t
of node
m
. We want to apply Lemma
C.1
.

From the definition of
\Hd^{b}_{p,k}
and point 1 of Lemma
B.1
(the coefficients are summable), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}}\Hd^{b}_{p,k}

\displaystyle=\sup_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}}\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}\left[\sum_{p^{\prime\prime}\in\bm{P}}b^{p^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime\prime}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k^{\prime}-}h^{p^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime\prime}}(k^{\prime}-k^{\prime\prime})d{D}^{p^{\prime\prime}}_{k^{\prime\prime}}\right]d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq P^{2}\sup_{p,p^{\prime},p^{\prime\prime}\in\bm{P}}(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}_{p,p^{\prime}}b^{p^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime\prime}}\|h^{p^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime\prime}}\|^{\infty}_{1,n_{p^{\prime\prime}}}.

Thus it is clear that
\sup_{p,k}\Hd^{b}_{p,k}<\infty
. From assumption
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec1}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
, we have
\|h^{m}_{p}\|_{1,n_{p}}^{\infty}<\infty
for all
m,p\in\bm{M},\bm{P}
, thus we have

{i}_{m}:=\sup_{t\in\mathds{R}}\left(\mu_{m}+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t-}\left[h^{m}_{p}(t-k)\times(a^{m}_{p}\Hd^{b}_{p,k}+b^{m}_{p})\right]d{D}^{p}_{k}\right)<\infty.

(10.4)

Recall that
\Hd^{\xi}_{p,k}=\displaystyle\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}\xi^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}
. Thus it follows that

\mathcal{I}^{m}_{t}\leq{i}_{m}+\sum_{(p^{\prime},k^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Q}}A^{m}_{p^{\prime},k^{\prime}}(t)\xi^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}

(10.5)

with
A^{m}_{p^{\prime},k^{\prime}}(t)=\displaystyle\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t-}\mathds{1}_{k^{\prime}\leq k<t}a^{m}_{p}h^{m}_{p}(t-k)\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p}_{k}
.

Let
t\in\mathds{R}
,
p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}
and
m\in\bm{M}
, applying point 1 of Lemma
B.1
leads to,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}A^{m}_{p^{\prime},k^{\prime}}(t)d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t-}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k}a^{m}_{p}h^{m}_{p}(t-k)\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t-}a^{m}_{p}h^{m}_{p}(t-k)(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}_{p,p^{\prime}}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}a^{m}_{p}\|h^{m}_{p}\|^{\infty}_{1,n_{p}}(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}_{p,p^{\prime}}.

Finally we obtain

\sup_{t\in\mathds{R}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}A^{m}_{p^{\prime},k^{\prime}}(t)d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}<\infty.

(10.6)

With the notation of Lemma
C.1
we have

A_{\infty}:=\Big(\sup_{t\in\mathds{R}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}A^{m}_{p^{\prime},k^{\prime}}(t)d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}\Big)_{p^{\prime},m\in\bm{P},\bm{M}}

Thus we proved that

A_{\infty}\preceq\big[(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}\big]^{\intercal}\big(a^{m}_{p}\|h^{m}_{p}\|^{\infty}_{1,n_{p}}\big)_{p,m\in\bm{P},\bm{M}}.

The intensity fits now the framework of Lemma
C.1
.

For exponential moments of
F\bullet S
we can just apply Lemma
C.1
with
B^{m}=F^{m}
and
C^{p}=0
. The result follows immediately since by assumption
\bm{(}\text{{{SubG}}}\bm{)}
,
K_{1}:=\max_{p\in\bm{P}}\max(\mathfrak{e}_{p},\mathfrak{s}_{p})<\infty
, and one can take
K:=\max\big(K_{1},\max_{m}{i}_{m}\big)
.

For exponential moments of
F\bullet W
we need to work on the expression of
F\bullet W
. Recall from Theorem
4.5
the expression of
W^{p}_{k}
,

W^{p}_{k}=\Hd^{b}_{p,k}+\Hd^{\xi}_{p,k}+\sum_{m}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k-}\Hd^{m}_{p,k}(t)dS^{m}_{t}.

Thus we can decompose
F\bullet W
as follows,

\displaystyle F\bullet W=\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int F^{p}(k)\Hd^{b}_{p,k}d{D}^{p}_{k}+

\displaystyle\sum_{p,p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int\displaystyle\int F^{p}(k)\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}\xi^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int\sum_{p,p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int\displaystyle\int F^{p}(k)\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}h^{p^{\prime}}_{m}(k^{\prime}-t)d{D}^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}dS^{m}_{t}.

Thus we we have

F\bullet W\leq K|F^{\bm{P}}_{\infty}|_{1}+\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int C^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}\xi^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int B^{m}(t)dS^{m}_{t}

(10.7)

with
K<\infty
depending on
\mathfrak{M}
only and,

\displaystyle C^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}=\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int F^{p}(k)\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p}_{k},

\displaystyle B^{m}(t)=\sum_{p,p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int\displaystyle\int F^{p}(k)\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}h^{p^{\prime}}_{m}(k^{\prime}-t)d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p}_{k}.

Let
n\in\mathds{N}^{*}
. With the notation of Lemma
C.1
we have to bound
C_{\infty}
and
B_{\infty,n}
. One has,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int C^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int\displaystyle\int F^{p}(k)\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int\displaystyle\int F^{p}(k)(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}_{p,p^{\prime}}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\big[(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}\big]^{\intercal}F^{\bm{P}}_{\infty}\big)_{p^{\prime}}.

Thus we have
C_{\infty}\preceq\big[(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}\big]^{\intercal}F^{\bm{P}}_{\infty}
.

For
B_{\infty,n}
we have,

\displaystyle(B_{\infty,n})_{m}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p,p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int\displaystyle\int F^{p}(k)\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}\|h^{p^{\prime}}_{m}\|^{\infty}_{1,1/n}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p,p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int F^{p}(k)(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}_{p,p^{\prime}}\|h^{p^{\prime}}_{m}\|^{\infty}_{1,1/n}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p,p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}(F^{\bm{P}}_{\infty})_{p}(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}_{p,p^{\prime}}\|h^{p^{\prime}}_{m}\|^{\infty}_{1,1/n}.

Which proves that
B_{\infty,n}\preceq R_{n}F^{\bm{P}}_{\infty}
. Similarly, applying Lemma
C.1
concludes.
∎

10.3
Proof of Corollary
5.5
and Proposition
5.6

Under assumption
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec2}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}^{+}]
, Theorem 3.1 of
[
41
]
shows that there exists
\theta_{0}>0
and
\theta_{1}<\infty
depending on
\mathfrak{M}
such that if
|u|_{\infty}\leq\theta_{0}
then
|\bm{{L}}(u)|_{\infty}\leq\theta_{1}
. By Jensen inequality we have
\bm{{L}}(tu)\preceq t\bm{{L}}(u)
for all
0\leq t\leq 1
. It follows that if
|u|_{\infty}\theta_{0}^{-1}=\beta\leq 1
, we have

\big|e^{\bm{{L}}(u)}-\bm{1}\big|_{\infty}\leq\theta_{1}e^{\theta_{1}}\beta.

(10.8)

We apply Theorem
5.3
to the HAR process
\bm{{\HAR}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}\bm{(}-\infty\bm{)}
, which dominates, by Proposition
5.2
, the HAR process
\bm{{\HAR}}_{\mathfrak{M}}\bm{(}-\infty\bm{)}
. From the definition of
\mathfrak{M}^{+}
it is clear that the matrices
R_{n},A_{\infty}
calculated in Corollary
5.5
with
\mathfrak{M}
and in Theorem
5.3
with
\mathfrak{M}^{+}
coincide. Finally one can check, using (
10.8
), the following bounds,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{X}^{\bm{M}}_{n}|_{1}\leq K_{1}\beta n,

\displaystyle|A_{\infty}\mathcal{X}^{\bm{M}}_{n}|_{1}\leq K_{2}\beta n,

\displaystyle|A_{\infty}\mathcal{X}^{\bm{M}}_{n}|_{2}^{2}\leq K_{3}\beta^{2}n^{2},

with
K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}
constants depending on
\bm{M}
, and similarly for
\mathcal{X}^{\bm{P}}_{n}
, which concludes the proof.
∎

The fact that
\spr(\tilde{H}_{\mathfrak{M}})<1\Rightarrow\text{\hyperlink{spec}{$\bm{(}\text{{{Spec1}}}\bm{)}$}}[\mathfrak{M}]
is a consequence of
\tilde{H}_{\mathfrak{M}}\succeq H_{\mathfrak{M}}
. Indeed, for matrix with non negative entries the spectral radius is increasing with the entries. Thus
\spr(\tilde{H}_{\mathfrak{M}})\geq\spr(H_{\mathfrak{M}})
, which implies
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec1}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
. For
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec2}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}^{+}]
it is a direct consequence of (
5.1
), stating that

\mathfrak{H}_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}\preceq H^{S}_{S}+H^{S}_{W}(\Id-H_{W}^{W})^{-1}\tilde{H}^{W}_{S},

and the following applied to
\tilde{H}_{\mathfrak{M}}
.

For any matrix
N=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}
with non negative entries, if
\spr(N)<1
then we have

\spr\Big(A+B(\Id-D)^{-1}C=A+B\sum_{n\in\mathds{N}}D^{n}C\Big)\leq\spr(N).

Indeed, one can check that for all
i\in\mathds{N}
we have the following

\sum_{j\geq i}\bigg(A+B\sum_{n\in\mathds{N}}D^{n}C\bigg)^{j}\leq\begin{pmatrix}\Id&0\end{pmatrix}\bigg[\sum_{j\geq i}N^{j}\bigg]\begin{pmatrix}\Id\\
0\end{pmatrix}.

Thus one concludes with the following result: For any square matrix
R
with non negative coefficients, for any norm, we have

\forall 0\leq x<1,\quad\spr(R)\leq x\iff\forall\varepsilon>0,\ \limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\big\|\sum_{j\geq n}R^{j}\big\|}{(x+\varepsilon)^{n}}=0.

∎

11
Proof of results of Section
6

11.1
Proof of Proposition
6.1

Let
\mathfrak{M}_{c}
be parameters being equal to
\mathfrak{M}
except that
h^{p}_{p^{\prime}}
is replaced by
x\mapsto e^{cx}h^{p}_{p^{\prime}}(x)
for all
p,p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}
where
c>0
will be chosen later. Then, from the closed form of the coefficients (see Proposition
4.4
) it is clear that we have

\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}_{c}}=e^{c(k-k^{\prime})}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}.

(11.1)

Considering assumption
\bm{(}\text{{{ExpTail}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
, we have that the function

c\mapsto\|e^{c\id}h^{p}_{p^{\prime}}\|^{\infty}_{1,n_{p^{\prime}}}

is continuous on a open set containing
0
. Thus, since the spectral radius is a continuous function of the entries of the matrix,
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec1.1}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
ensures that
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec1.1}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}_{c}]
holds for
c
small enough. Thus one can apply point 1 of Lemma
B.1
and find that

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}_{c}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}\leq K<\infty

with
K
(which depends on
\mathfrak{M}
and
c
) uniform over
(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}
. The first result follows from (
11.1
).

For the cluster functions, the result is a direct consequence of
\bm{(}\text{{{ExpTail}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
, exponential decay of the coefficients we just proved and the expression of the clusters functions given in Definition
4.7
. Indeed, for
t>s
, one has (the constants
K,c>0
might change from line to line),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(s,t)

\displaystyle=h^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(t-s)+\sum_{p,p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{s+}^{t-}\displaystyle\int_{s+}^{k}a^{m}_{p}h^{m}_{p}(t-k)\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}h^{p^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime}}(k^{\prime}-s)d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{c(t-s)}+\sum_{p,p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{s+}^{t-}\displaystyle\int_{s+}^{k}Ke^{c(t-k)}Ke^{c(k-k^{\prime})}Ke^{c(k^{\prime}-s)}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{c(t-s)}+Ke^{c(t-s)}\sum_{p,p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{s+}^{t-}\displaystyle\int_{s+}^{k}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{c(t-s)}+K(t-s)^{2}e^{c(t-s)}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{c(t-s)}.

∎

11.2
Proof of Theorem
6.3

In this subsection we prove Theorem
6.3
. Along this subsection we fix some parameters

\mathfrak{M}=\big((\mu_{m})_{m},\ (h^{\alpha}_{\beta})_{\alpha,\beta},\ (\Phi^{\alpha})_{\alpha},\ (\kappa^{\alpha}_{p})_{\alpha,p}\big),

a time
t_{0}\in\mathds{R}
and two integrable initial conditions for
\mathfrak{M}
at time
t_{0}
,
\mathfrak{C}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{C}_{2}
. We suppose that assumptions
\bm{(}\text{{{NoExpl}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
,
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec1.1}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
,
\bm{(}\text{{{ExpTail}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
and
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec2}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}^{+}]
hold. In the sequel,
K
and
c
are positive constants depending only on
\mathfrak{M}
. Usually
K
is intended to be large and
c
small. They might change from line to line.

We denote by

\Big((\lambda^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{i}})_{m\in\bm{M}},(S^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{i}})_{m\in\bm{M}},(W^{p,\mathfrak{C}_{i}})_{p\in\bm{P}}\Big)

the unique non exploding
\bm{{\HAR}}_{\mathfrak{M}}\bm{(}t_{0},\mathfrak{C}_{i}\bm{)}
process for
i=1,2
(Theorem
3.4
applies).
We define the deviation process

\Big((\Delta\lambda^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},(\Delta S^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},(\Delta W^{p})_{p\in\bm{P}}\Big)
by

•

For
t\geq t_{0}
let

\Delta\lambda^{m}(t)=|\lambda^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{1}}_{t}-\lambda^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{2}}_{t}|.

•

For
t\geq t_{0}
let

d\Delta S^{m}(t)=|dS^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{1}}_{t}-dS^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{2}}_{t}|=\pi^{m}\Big(\{t\}\times\big(\min(\lambda^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{1}}_{t},\lambda^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{2}}_{t}),\max(\lambda^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{1}}_{t},\lambda^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{2}}_{t})\big]\Big).

•

For
(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}
with
k\geq t_{0}
let

\Delta W^{p}(k)=|W^{p,\mathfrak{C}_{1}}_{k}-W^{p,\mathfrak{C}_{2}}_{k}|.

Lemma
D.1
in Appendix
D
dominates the deviation process by a linear HAR process with parameters
\mathfrak{M}^{+}
and with vanishing base rates. To avoid any confusion, we denote
g^{\alpha}_{\beta}=L_{\alpha}|h^{\alpha}_{\beta}|
the interaction functions of
\mathfrak{M}^{+}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{m}_{m^{\prime}}
the cluster functions associated to
\mathfrak{M}^{+}
defined in Definition
4.7
. Lemma
D.1
states the following.

There exist Poisson random measures
{P}^{m}
for
m\in\bm{M}
on
[t_{0},\infty)\times\mathds{R}_{+}
, independent of
\mathcal{F}_{t_{0}-}
and a process
\Big((\mathcal{L}^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},({S}^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},({W}^{p})_{p\in\bm{P}}\Big)
adapted (and previsible for
\mathcal{L}
) to the filtration
\mathcal{G}_{t}=\sigma\big(\mathcal{F}_{t_{0}-},{P}_{|[t_{0},t]\times\mathds{R}_{+}}\big)
such that,

•

for
m\in\bm{M},\ t\geq t_{0}
,
\Delta\lambda^{m}(t)\leq\mathcal{L}^{m}_{t}
,

•

for
m\in\bm{M},\ t\geq t_{0}
,
d\Delta S^{m}(t)\leq d{S}^{m}_{t}
,

•

for
(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q},\ k\geq t_{0}
,
\Delta W^{p}(k)\leq{W}^{p}_{k}
.

And we have, for
t\geq t_{0}
,
C\subset[t_{0},\infty)
and
(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}
with
k\geq t_{0}
,

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}^{m}_{t}=u_{0}e^{-c(t-t_{0})}+\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t-}\mathfrak{g}^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(s,t)d{S}^{m^{\prime}}_{s}\\
&{S}^{m}(C)=\displaystyle\int_{C\times\mathds{R}_{+}}\mathds{1}_{0<x\leq\mathcal{L}^{m}_{s}}d{P}^{m}(s,x)\\
&{W}^{p}_{k}=u_{0}e^{-c(k-t_{0})}+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{k-}Ke^{-c(k-s)}d{S}^{m}_{s}\end{aligned}\right.

where
u_{0}=K|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}
with
K,c,\delta>0
constants depending only on
\mathfrak{M}
.

The process
\Big((\mathcal{L}^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},({S}^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},({W}^{p})_{p\in\bm{P}}\Big)
is quite similar to a
\bm{{\HAR}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}\bm{(}t_{0}\bm{)}
process. The main difference is that the immigration rate of the point processes is not constant nor 1-stationary as in Theorem
4.5
, it vanishes exponentially fast. Thus one can have the following reasoning: since the immigrant rate is vanishing, at some point there will be no more cluster roots and thus no more points when all the clusters will have ended.

Let
T_{e}
the extinction time of
({S}^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}}
. It is defined as the time of the last point,

T_{e}=\max_{m\in\bm{M}}\sup\ \{t\geq t_{0}\mid d{S}^{m}_{t}=1\}.

Let us prove the following result.

Proposition 11.1
.

There exists
K,c>0
constants depending only on
\mathfrak{M}
such that

\forall t\geq 0,\ \mathds{P}\left(T_{e}\leq t_{0}+t\right)\geq 1-K\mathds{E}\left[|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}\right]e^{-ct}.

First let us work conditionally to
u_{0}
, so that the immigration rate is deterministic. We use the cluster representation. Denote by
\Pi^{m}
the immigrant points from node
m
. We use the same notations as in Section
4.3
so that

\Pi^{m}=\{s\in[t_{0},\infty)\mid(\varnothing,m,s)\ \text{is the root of a cluster}\}.

For an immigrant point
(\varnothing,m,s)
we denote by
\tau(m,s)
the (temporal) length of the cluster it generates. We emphasize that the law of the length of the cluster depends on
s
since the interacting functions
\mathfrak{g}(s,t)
depend on both
s
and
t
and not just on
t-s
. We also denote by
\tau^{m}(s)
a random variable with law being the length of a cluster born at time
s
and with a root of type
m
.

Conditionally to
u_{0}
we have,

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\left[\mathds{1}_{T_{e}\leq t_{0}+t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}\left[\prod_{m\in\bm{M}}\prod_{s\in\Pi^{m}}\mathds{1}_{\tau(m,s)\leq t_{0}+t-s}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}\left[\prod_{m\in\bm{M}}\prod_{s\in\Pi^{m}}\mathds{P}\left(\tau^{m}(s)\leq t_{0}+t-s\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}\left[\exp\left(\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}\log\big[\mathds{P}\left(\tau^{m}(s)\leq t_{0}+t-s\right)\big]d\Pi^{m}_{s}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}\big(\mathds{P}\left(\tau^{m}(s)\leq t_{0}+t-s\right)-1\big)u_{0}e^{-c(s-t_{0})}ds\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}\mathds{P}\left(\tau^{m}(s)>t_{0}+t-s\right)u_{0}e^{-c(s-t_{0})}ds\right)

with
u_{0}e^{-c(s-t_{0})}
the immigrants rate.

Let
G^{m}_{s}
a cluster born at time
s
with root of type
m
, then for
t_{0}+t\geq s
we have

\displaystyle\mathds{P}\left(\tau^{m}(s)>t_{0}+t-s\right)

\displaystyle=\mathds{P}\left(G^{m}_{s}([s+t_{0}+t-s,\infty))\geq 1\right)

\displaystyle=\mathds{P}\left(e^{\theta_{2}\card(G^{m}_{s}\cap[s+t_{0}+t-s,\infty))}-1\geq e^{\theta_{2}}-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{-c(t_{0}+t-s)}

where in the last step we use Markov inequality and Lemma
6.2
(which applies since
\bm{(}\text{{{ExpTail}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
implies
\bm{(}\text{{{ExpTail}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}^{+}]
). If
s>t_{0}+t
then we trivially have
\mathds{P}\left(\tau^{m}(s)>t_{0}+t-s\right)=1
. It follows that

\mathds{P}\left(T_{e}\leq t_{0}+t\right)\geq\exp\left(-\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{0}+t}u_{0}Ke^{-c(t_{0}+t-s)}e^{-c(s-t_{0})}ds-\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}+t}^{\infty}u_{0}e^{-c(s-t_{0})}ds\right).

From this point easy calculations leads to

\mathds{P}\left(T_{e}\leq t\right)\geq\exp\left(-u_{0}Ke^{-ct}\right)\geq 1-u_{0}Ke^{-ct}.

It only remains to integrate with respect to
u_{0}=K|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}
since we were conditionally to
u_{0}
, which concludes the proof of Proposition
11.1
.
∎

Proposition
11.1
is exactly the first point of Theorem
6.3
for the point process part. To control the chains, we need bounds on the total number of points. Let
{S}_{\infty}=\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}{S}^{m}([t_{0},\infty))
the total number of points. Let us prove that moments of
{S}_{\infty}
are linked to moments of
|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}
. More precisely we have the following.

Proposition 11.2
.

For all
q\geq 1
, there exists
K_{q}
depending on
q
and
\mathfrak{M}
such that

\mathds{E}\left[{S}_{\infty}^{q}\right]\leq K_{q}\mathds{E}\left[|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}+|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}^{q}\right].

We use the same notations as in Proposition
11.1
, we denote by
\Pi^{m}
all the immigrant points from node
m\in\bm{M}
. For
s\in\Pi^{m}
, the cluster generated by
(\varnothing,m,s)
is
G^{m}_{s}
. As in the proof of Proposition
11.1
we work conditionally to
u_{0}
and integrate with respect to
u_{0}
at the end. Let
\mathcal{A}^{m}
be
{P}\!{o}\!{i}\!{s}(\mathfrak{H}_{\mathfrak{M}^{+}}^{\intercal})
Galton Watson process with root of type
m\in\bm{M}
. As explains in Section
4.3
, for any
s\in\Pi^{m}
and any
m\in\bm{M}
, there exists a coupling such that
\mathcal{A}^{m}
dominates stochastically
G^{m}_{s}
.

Since conditionally to the immigrants the clusters are independent we have,

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\left[{S}_{\infty}^{q}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}\left[\mathds{E}\left[\bigg(\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\sum_{s\in\Pi^{m}}\card(G^{m}_{s})\bigg)^{q}\ \middle|\ (\Pi^{m})_{m}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathds{E}\left[\bigg(\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\sum_{s\in\Pi^{m}}\mathds{E}\left[\card(G^{m}_{s})^{q}\right]^{1/q}\bigg)^{q}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}\left[\bigg(\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\card(\Pi^{m})\mathds{E}\left[\card(\mathcal{A}^{m})^{q}\right]^{1/q}\bigg)^{q}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathds{E}\left[\card(\Pi)^{q}\right]\max_{m\in\bm{M}}\mathds{E}\left[\card(\mathcal{A}^{m})^{q}\right],

where we used Minkowski to obtain the second line and
\Pi=\bigcup_{m\in\bm{M}}\Pi^{m}
. Since conditionally to
u_{0}
,
\card(\Pi)
follows a law
{P}\!{o}\!{i}\!{s}(Mu_{0}/c)
where
u_{0}=K|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}
we have

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\left[\card(\Pi)^{q}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}\left[\mathds{E}\left[{P}\!{o}\!{i}\!{s}(Mu_{0}/c)^{q}\ \middle|\ u_{0}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq K_{q}\mathds{E}\big[|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}+|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}^{q}\big]

where
K_{q}
is a constant depending on
q
and
KM/c
(which depends on
\mathfrak{M}
) such that the moment of order
q
of the law
{P}\!{o}\!{i}\!{s}(\alpha KM/c)
is bounded by
K_{q}(\alpha+\alpha^{q})
. Which concludes the proof of Proposition
11.2
.
∎

Let us now focus on chains. Since
{W}^{p}_{k}
is a function of the point processes, we can use the results of Propositions
11.1
and
11.2
to obtain the following result.

Proposition 11.3
.

There exists
c,K>0
constants depending only on
\mathfrak{M}
such that for all
t\geq 0
we have

\mathds{P}\left(\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}+t}^{\infty}{W}^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}\leq e^{-ct}\right)\geq 1-K\mathds{E}\left[|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}\right]e^{-ct}.

Recall that

{W}^{p}_{k}=u_{0}e^{-c(k-t_{0})}+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{k-}Ke^{-c(k-s)}d{S}^{m}_{s}.

Thus

\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}+t}^{\infty}{W}^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}\leq Ku_{0}e^{-ct}+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}Ke^{-c(t_{0}+t-s)}d{S}^{m}_{s}.

And thus, if
{S}_{\infty}
is the total number of points,
T_{e}
the extinction time then one has

\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}+t}^{\infty}{W}^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}\leq Ku_{0}e^{-ct}+K{S}_{\infty}e^{-c(t_{0}+t-T_{e})}.

From Proposition
11.1
we have

\mathds{P}\left(T_{e}>t_{0}+t/4\right)\leq K^{\prime}\mathds{E}\left[|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}\right]e^{-c^{\prime}t}.

From Proposition
11.2
and Markov inequality

\mathds{P}\left({S}_{\infty}>e^{ct/4}/2K\right)\leq K^{\prime}\mathds{E}\left[|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}\right]e^{-c^{\prime}t}.

Finally, Markov inequality also gives

\mathds{P}\left(u_{0}>e^{ct/2}/2K\right)\leq K^{\prime}\mathds{E}\left[|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}\right]e^{-c^{\prime}t}.

Combining previous inequalities gives

\mathds{P}\left(Ku_{0}e^{-ct}+K{S}_{\infty}e^{-c(t_{0}+t-T_{e})}>e^{-ct/2}\right)\leq K^{\prime}\mathds{E}\left[|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}\right]e^{-c^{\prime}t},

and thus,

\mathds{P}\left(\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}+t}^{\infty}{W}^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}>e^{-c/2t}\right)\leq K^{\prime}\mathds{E}\left[|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}\right]e^{-c^{\prime}t}.

Which concludes the proof of Proposition
11.3
up to reducing either
c
or
c^{\prime}
so that
c/2=c^{\prime}
.
∎

The proof of Theorem
6.3
follows easily from this point.

The first point of Theorem
6.3
is exactly Propositions
11.1
and
11.3
. Let us focus on the almost sure convergences. We have

\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\card\big((S^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{1}}\vartriangle S^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{2}})\cap[t+t_{0},\infty)\big)\leq{S}_{\infty}\mathds{1}_{T_{e}\leq t_{0}+t}

where
{S}_{\infty}
is the total number of points of
{S}
and
T_{e}
the extinction time. Since from Proposition
11.2
the expectation of
{S}_{\infty}
is finite, it follows that
{S}_{\infty}<\infty
almost surely and thus
T_{e}<\infty
almost surely which concludes the almost sure convergence. For the chains, from the proof of Proposition
11.3
we have

\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}+t}^{\infty}|W^{p,\mathfrak{C}_{1}}_{k}-W^{p,\mathfrak{C}_{2}}_{k}|d{D}^{p}_{k}\leq u_{0}Ke^{-ct}+K{S}_{\infty}e^{-c(t_{0}+t-T_{e})}.

Finiteness of
T_{e},\ {S}_{\infty}
and
u_{0}
leads to the almost sure convergence. It remains the
L^{q}
convergences. They are simple consequences of the following statement.

\forall 1\leq q<q_{0},\ \forall x,z\geq 0,\ \ \Big(\mathds{P}\left(Y>x\right)\leq z\ \Longrightarrow\ \mathds{E}\left[Y^{q}\right]\leq x^{q}+z^{1-q/q_{0}}\mathds{E}\left[Y^{q_{0}}\right]^{q/q_{0}}\Big)

(11.2)

where
q_{0}>1
and
Y
is non negative random variable. We apply (
11.2
) to

Y=Y_{t}=\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\card\big((S^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{1}}\vartriangle S^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{2}})\cap[t+t_{0},\infty)\big)

with
x=x_{t}=e^{-ct}
and
z=z_{t}=K\mathds{E}\left[|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}\right]e^{-ct}
. Since by assumption
\mathds{E}\left[|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}^{q_{0}}\right]<\infty
it is clear by Proposition
11.2
that
Y_{t}\leq{S}_{\infty}\in L^{q_{0}}
uniformly in
t
, which gives the result.
Similarly, for the chains we apply (
11.2
), with the same
x_{t}
and
z_{t}
, to

Y_{t}=\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}+t}^{\infty}|W^{p,\mathfrak{C}_{1}}_{k}-W^{p,\mathfrak{C}_{2}}_{k}|d{D}^{p}_{k}\leq u_{0}+K{S}_{\infty}\in L^{q_{0}},

where the bounds in
L^{q_{0}}
, uniform in
t
, are from Proposition
11.2
. Which concludes on the
L^{q}
convergences and finishes the proof.

11.3
Proof of Corollary
6.4
and Propositions
6.6
and
6.7

To prove Corollary
6.4
we apply Theorem
6.3
with the initial conditions
\mathfrak{C}^{-\infty}
and
\mathfrak{C}^{\varnothing}
. The first one is given by the stationary HAR process on
(-\infty,t_{0})
and the second one is the empty one. From this choice, it is clear that the processes of interest are, respectively, the stationary process and the process started at time
t_{0}
. Let
\delta>0
. We need to prove that
|\mathfrak{C}^{-\infty},\mathfrak{C}^{\varnothing}|_{\delta}
has a finite mean and, under
\bm{(}\text{{{SubG}}}\bm{)}
, moments of any order. We have

|\mathfrak{C}^{-\infty},\mathfrak{C}^{\varnothing}|_{\delta}=\max\left(\left[\max_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t_{0}-}e^{-\delta(t_{0}-s)}dS^{m,-\infty}_{s}\right],\ \left[\max_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t_{0}-}e^{-\delta(t_{0}-k)}(1+|W^{p,-\infty}_{k}|)d{D}^{p}_{k}\right]\right).

Thus from Theorem
3.9
it is clear that there is a finite expectation since
\sup_{m\in\bm{M},\ t\in\mathds{R}}\mathds{E}\left[\lambda^{m,-\infty}_{t}\right]<\infty
and
\sup_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathds{E}\left[|W^{p,-\infty}_{k}|\right]<\infty
.

If
\bm{(}\text{{{SubG}}}\bm{)}
holds then Corollary
5.5
applies and
|\mathfrak{C}^{-\infty},\mathfrak{C}^{\varnothing}|_{\delta}
has exponential moments (thus moments of any order) regardless of
\delta>0
. Corollary
6.4
follows directly from Theorem
6.3
.
∎

The process
\mathbb{X}^{(n,\ell,\phi)}{\Big|}_{[jn+\phi,\infty)}
only depends on processes
\HAR_{\mathfrak{M}}(in+\phi-\ell)
with
i\geq j
. Thus it is clear that
\mathbb{X}^{(n,\ell,\phi)}{\Big|}_{[jn+\phi,\infty)}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{s}
for any
s<jn+\phi-\ell
, leading to the result.

For the second result, let

Y_{j}=\mathbb{X}^{(n,\ell,\phi)}{\Big|}_{[jn+\phi,(j+1)n+\phi+(n-\ell))}.

It is clear that
Y_{j}
is measurable for
\mathcal{F}_{((j+1)n+\phi+(n-\ell))-}
, and, by the first part, independent of
\mathcal{F}_{(jn+\phi-\ell)-}
. Since

jn+\phi-\ell=((j-2)+1)n+\phi+(n-\ell),

it is clear that
Y_{j}
is independent of
Y_{j-2}
and any
(Y_{i})_{i\leq j-2}
. Since
j
is arbitrary it concludes.
∎

This result is straightforward from Corollary
6.4
since we are comparing the processes
\bm{{\HAR}}_{\mathfrak{M}}\bm{(}-\infty\bm{)}
and
\bm{{\HAR}}_{\mathfrak{M}}\bm{(}t_{0}\bm{)}
with
t_{0}=jn+\phi-\ell
on the interval
[t_{0}+\ell,t_{0}+\ell+n]
.
∎

12
Proofs of results of Section
7

We recall the in Section
7
the observed process is the
\bm{{\HAR}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\star}}\bm{(}-\infty\bm{)}
process from Theorem
3.9
and that assumptions
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec1}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}^{\star}]
,
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec2}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}^{\star,+}]
,
\bm{(}\text{{{ExpTail}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}^{\star}]
,
\bm{(}\text{{{FiMe}}}\bm{)}
and
\bm{(}\text{{{SubG}}}\bm{)}
hold. In the sequel
C^{\star}
usually stands for a large constant depending on
\mathfrak{M}^{\star}
, while
c^{\star}
stands for a small constant depending on
\mathfrak{M}^{\star}
. They might change from line to line.

12.1
Proof of Theorem
7.9
and Proposition
7.11

The proof of Theorem
7.9
is based on Lemmas
E.2
and
E.3
given in Appendix
E
. These lemmas generalize Bernstein inequality to HAR processes.

Let
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{M}}
, from Lemma
E.2
we have that for any
x>0
and
q>0

|b_{\varphi}-\tilde{b}_{\varphi}|=|J_{\varphi,\bm{M}}|\leq B_{\varphi}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{3x(x+C^{\star}(q+1)T\log(T))}{3-e}}+\frac{x}{3}\Big)

with
B_{\varphi}=1
and probability at least

1-4e^{-x}\frac{\log(1+C^{\star}(q+1)T\log(T)/x)}{\log(3/2)}-\frac{C^{\star}}{T^{q}}.

Let us apply this result with
x=(q+1)\log(T)>0
since
T\geq 2
. Then the bound becomes

|b_{\varphi}-\tilde{b}_{\varphi}|\leq C^{\star}(q+1)\log(T)\sqrt{T}

and the probability is lower bounded by
1-C^{\star}/T^{q}
since

\displaystyle 4e^{-x}\frac{\log(1+C^{\star}(q+1)T\log(T)/x)}{\log(3/2)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T^{q}}\times\Big(\frac{4\log(1+C^{\star}T)}{T\log(3/2)}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\star}}{T^{q}}.

For the other cases,
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}^{\bm{M}}
,
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{P}}
or
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}^{\bm{P}}
the proof follows the same argument: applying Lemma
E.2
or Lemma
E.3
with
x=(q+1)\log(T)
.
∎

Let
x
and
J
such that
|J|\leq s
and
|x_{J^{c}}|_{1}\leq c|x_{J}|_{1}
. Then we have

\displaystyle x^{\intercal}Vx

\displaystyle=x^{\intercal}Ux+x^{\intercal}(V-U)x

\displaystyle\geq\eta\|x_{J}\|^{2}-\varepsilon|x|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\eta\|x_{J}\|^{2}-\varepsilon(c+1)^{2}|x_{J}|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\eta\|x_{J}\|^{2}-\varepsilon(c+1)^{2}s\|x_{J}\|^{2}

where we use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to obtain the last line. This concludes the proof.
∎

12.2
Proof of Lemma
7.12

Let
n\geq 2A+1
an integer, and
\tau\in\mathds{N}^{*}
such that

T=2n\tau+r

with
0\leq r<2n
. Let
\mathbb{X}^{(n,\ell=n-A,\phi=-A)}
the coupling from Definition
6.5
. For readability we drop the indexes and denote
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{X}^{(n,n-A,-A)}
. Since
n\geq 2A+1
we have
\ell\geq n/2
.

In this proof we will need to bound the HAR process
X
over all possible sliding windows of size
A
in the interval
[-A,T)
, to do so, we define
Y_{T}
as follows. Let for
m\in\bm{M}
,

Y^{m}(T)=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{intervals}\ I\subset[-A,T)\\
\text{such that}\ |I|\leq A\end{subarray}}S^{m}(I)

and similarly, for
p\in\bm{P}
and
\alpha\in\bm{M}\cup\bm{P}
,

Y^{\alpha}_{p}(T)=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{intervals}\ I\subset[-A,T)\\
\text{such that}\ |I|\leq A\end{subarray}}\displaystyle\int_{I}|\kappa^{\alpha}_{p}(W^{p}_{k})|d{D}^{p}_{k}.

Finally, let
Y_{T}=\max\big(\max_{m}Y^{m}(T),\ \max_{\alpha,p}Y^{\alpha}_{p}(T)\big)
. Proposition
E.1
, in Appendix
E
, bounds
Y_{T}
as follows,

\forall x\geq 0,\quad\mathds{P}\left(Y_{T}\leq x\right)\geq 1-C^{\star}Te^{-c^{\star}x}.

(12.1)

It also bounds
\mathbb{Y}_{T}
, defined exactly like
Y_{T}
but with
\mathbb{X}
instead of
X
, in the same way.

Let us now start the proof. Recall that

G_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}=\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T-}\Psi^{m}(\varphi,t,X_{t-A,t})\Psi^{m}(\varphi^{\prime},t,X_{t-A,t})dt+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T-}\Psi^{p}(\varphi,k,X_{k-A,k})\Psi^{p}(\varphi^{\prime},k,X_{k-A,k})d{D}^{p}_{k}.

Define

•

for
t_{0}<t_{1}
,

(G^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}:=\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}-}\Psi^{m}(\varphi,t,X)\Psi^{m}(\varphi^{\prime},t,X)dt+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}-}\Psi^{p}(\varphi,k,X)\Psi^{p}(\varphi^{\prime},k,X)d{D}^{p}_{k}

(12.2)

and

(\mathbb{G}^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}:=\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}-}\Psi^{m}(\varphi,t,\mathbb{X})\Psi^{m}(\varphi^{\prime},t,\mathbb{X})dt+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}-}\Psi^{p}(\varphi,k,\mathbb{X})\Psi^{p}(\varphi^{\prime},k,\mathbb{X})d{D}^{p}_{k}

•

For integers
1\leq j\leq\tau
,

(\mathcal{G}^{\text{even}}_{j})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}:=\big(\mathbb{G}^{(2j-1)n}_{2(j-1)n}\big)_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[\big(\mathbb{G}^{(2j-1)n}_{2(j-1)n}\big)_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]

and

(\mathcal{G}^{\text{odd}}_{j})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}:=\big(\mathbb{G}^{2jn}_{(2j-1)n}\big)_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[\big(\mathbb{G}^{2jn}_{(2j-1)n}\big)_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right].

Considering the support assumption, for any
t_{0}<t_{1}
, the matrix
\mathbb{G}^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}
is measurable with respect to
\mathbb{X}_{t_{0}-A,t_{1}}
. The choice of the coupling
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{X}^{n,n-A,-A}
is tuned so that, by Proposition
6.6
, the random variables
\mathcal{G}^{\text{even}}_{j},\ j=1,\cdots,\tau
are iid and similarly
\mathcal{G}^{\text{odd}}_{j},\ j=1,\cdots,\tau
are also iid.

Our goal is to control
|G_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[G_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]|
. Thus we introduce our coupling to reveal independence to be able to apply a concentration result. First let us write

\displaystyle\big|G_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[G_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]\big|\leq

\displaystyle\big|(G_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\big|

(12.3)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]\big|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\big|\mathds{E}\left[(G_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]\big|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\big|(G^{T}_{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[(G^{T}_{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]\big|.

The ideas are the following: the first term is small since the coupling
\mathbb{X}
is close to the stationary process
X
, the second term is small since it is equal to

\sum_{j=1}^{\tau}(\mathcal{G}^{\text{even}}_{j})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}+\sum_{j=1}^{\tau}(\mathcal{G}^{\text{odd}}_{j})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}

where both
(\mathcal{G}^{\text{even}}_{j})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}},\ j=1,\cdots,\tau
and
(\mathcal{G}^{\text{odd}}_{j})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}},\ j=1,\cdots,\tau
are iid centered. The third term is small since
\mathbb{X}
is also close to
X
in
L^{2}
. Finally, the last term is also relatively small since it is a centered random variable with typical size
r<n
where the others have typical size
T
.

Proving Lemma
7.12
consists in controlling each of the four terms. The first term is controlled by the following result.

Proposition 12.1
.

For all
x\geq 0
we have

\mathds{P}\left(\forall\varphi,\varphi^{\prime},\ \big|(G_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\big|\leq Txe^{-c^{\star}n}\right)\geq 1-C^{\star}Te^{-c^{\star}x}-C^{\star}\tau e^{-c^{\star}n}.

In particular, we have

\mathds{P}\left(\forall\varphi,\varphi^{\prime},\ \big|(G_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\big|\leq C^{\star}Te^{-c^{\star}n}\right)\geq 1-C^{\star}\tau e^{-c^{\star}n}.

Recall that
\ell=n-A
and that
n\geq 2A+1
so that
\ell\geq n/2
. Remark that for
\alpha\in\bm{M}\cup\bm{P}
we have

\displaystyle|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,X)\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,X)-\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,\mathbb{X})\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,\mathbb{X})|\leq\

\displaystyle|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,X)|\times|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,X)-\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,\mathbb{X})|

(12.4)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,\mathbb{X})|\times|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,X)-\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,\mathbb{X})|.

Let
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}_{m}
with
m\in\bm{M}
, and
s\in[0,T)
. Then
\varphi_{m}
, the only non zero coordinate of
\varphi
is just a base rate for node
m
, ie just a constant. Then we have
\Psi^{m}(\varphi,s,X)=\varphi_{m}
. Since
\|\bm{\Phi}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
we have

|\Psi^{m}(\varphi,s,X)|=|\varphi_{m}|\leq 1.

Similarly, if
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}_{m}^{p}
with
m\in\bm{M}
and
p\in\bm{P}
, and
k\in[0,T)\cap\mathcal{D}_{p}
, then
\varphi
is an interaction function of node
m
on chain
p
. Thus we have

\Psi^{p}(\varphi,k,X)|=\displaystyle\int_{k-A}^{k-}\varphi^{p}_{m}(k-s)dS^{m}_{s}.

Since
\|\bm{\Phi}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
we have

|\Psi^{p}(\varphi,s,X)|\leq Y_{T}.

The same reasoning holds for any
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}
, any
\alpha\in\bm{M}\cup\bm{P}
and any
s\in[0,T)
, and we have

|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,X)|\leq 1+Y_{T}.

(12.5)

Similarly,

|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,\mathbb{X})|\leq 1+\mathbb{Y}_{T}.

(12.6)

Remark now that, if for example
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}^{\alpha}_{p}
, then

|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,X)-\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,\mathbb{X})|\leq\left|\displaystyle\int_{s-A}^{s-}\big(\kappa^{\alpha}_{p}(W^{p}_{k})-\kappa^{\alpha}_{p}(\mathbb{W}^{p}_{k})\big)d{D}^{p}_{k}\right|

and similarly there is a bound by the number of non common points between
X
and
\mathbb{X}
if
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}^{\alpha}_{m}
. For
j=0,\cdots,2\tau+1
define
Z_{j}
by

Z_{j}=\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\card\big(S^{m}\vartriangle\mathbb{S}^{m}\cap[jn-A,jn+n-A)\big)+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{jn-A}^{(jn+n-A)-}|W^{p}_{k}-\mathbb{W}^{p}_{k}|d{D}^{p}_{k}.

Thus, in all the cases, the following inequality holds for all
s\in[0,T)
,

|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,X)\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,X)-\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,\mathbb{X})\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,\mathbb{X})|\leq C^{\star}(1+Y_{T}+\mathbb{Y}_{T})\times\Big(\max_{j=0,\cdots,2\tau+1}Z_{j}\Big)

(12.7)

In (
12.1
), if
C^{\star}
is large enough (
C^{\star}\geq 1/T
) then the bound trivially holds for
x\leq 0
. So let us apply (
12.1
) to
Y_{T}
and
\mathbb{Y}_{T}
with
x-1/2
where
x\geq 0
. We also apply Proposition
6.7
to each of the
Z_{j}
to obtain

\mathds{P}\left(\sup_{s\in[0,T)}|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,X)\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,X)-\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,\mathbb{X})\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,\mathbb{X})|\leq C^{\star}xe^{-c^{\star}\ell}\right)\geq 1-C^{\star}Te^{-c^{\star}x}-C^{\star}\tau e^{-c^{\star}\ell}.

Since
\ell\geq n/2
, up to reducing
c^{\star}
we have

\mathds{P}\left(\sup_{s\in[0,T)}|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,X)\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,X)-\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,\mathbb{X})\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,\mathbb{X})|\leq C^{\star}xe^{-c^{\star}n}\right)\geq 1-C^{\star}Te^{-c^{\star}x}-C^{\star}\tau e^{-c^{\star}n}.

Finally, since

\big|(G_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\big|\leq(1+\max_{p}n_{p})\times T\times\sup_{s\in[0,T)}|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,X)\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,X)-\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,\mathbb{X})\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,\mathbb{X})|

we obtain the following

\mathds{P}\left(\big|(G_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\big|\leq C^{\star}Txe^{-c^{\star}n}\right)\geq 1-C^{\star}Te^{-c^{\star}x}-C^{\star}\tau e^{-c^{\star}n}.

The proof of the first result is complete up to the change of variable
\tilde{x}=C^{\star}x
. For the second inequality, take
x=n
and up to decreasing
c^{\star}
in the LHS, we have that
ne^{-c^{\star}n}\leq C^{\star}e^{-c^{\star}n}
. Remark also that since
T=2n\tau+r
, it is clear that
Te^{-c^{\star}n}\leq C^{\star}\tau e^{-c^{\star}n}
up to decreasing
c^{\star}
in the RHS, which concludes the proof.
∎

The next proposition deals with the second term and relies on Fuk-Nagaev concentration inequality taken from
[
44
]
.

Proposition 12.2
.

For all
q>2
there exists
C^{\star}_{q}
and
c^{\star}_{q}
constants that depend on both
\mathfrak{M}^{\star}
and
q
such that for all
x>0
,

\mathds{P}\Big(\big|(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]\big|\geq x\Big)\leq\frac{C^{\star}_{q}\tau n^{q}}{x^{q}}+4\exp\Big(-\frac{c^{\star}_{q}x^{2}}{\tau n^{2}}\Big).

With our notations,

(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]=\sum_{j=1}^{\tau}(\mathcal{G}^{\text{even}}_{j})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}+\sum_{j=1}^{\tau}(\mathcal{G}^{\text{odd}}_{j})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}

with
((\mathcal{G}^{\text{even}}_{j})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}})_{j}
iid random variables, as well as
((\mathcal{G}^{\text{odd}}_{j})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}})_{j}
and
(\mathcal{G}^{\text{even}}_{j})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\sim(\mathcal{G}^{\text{odd}}_{j^{\prime}})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}
.

Thus it is sufficient to look at

\mathds{P}\left(\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{\tau}(\mathcal{G}^{\text{even}}_{j})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\Big|\geq x/2\right).

To bound this probability we want to apply Fuk Nagaev inequality (Corollary 1.8 of
[
44
]
) given in Lemma
12.3
below.

Lemma 12.3

(Fuk-Nagaev)
.

Let
X_{1},\cdots,X_{n}
be centered and independent random variables. Let
S_{n}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}X_{k}
. Let also
M(q,n)=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathds{E}\left[|X_{k}|^{q}\right]
for
q\geq 2
and
\sigma_{n}^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathds{E}\left[X_{k}^{2}\right]=M(2,n)
. Then, for all
q>2
and
x>0
we have

\mathds{P}\left(|S_{n}|\geq x\right)\leq\dfrac{(1+2/q)^{q}M(q,n)}{x^{q}}+2\exp\left(-\dfrac{2x^{2}}{(q+2)^{2}e^{q}\sigma_{n}^{2}}\right).

To apply Lemma
12.3
we need to bound the moments of order
q
for
q>2
and moments of order
2
. Let
\tilde{q}\geq 1
. Since

(\mathcal{G}^{\text{even}}_{1})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(\mathbb{G}^{k+1}_{k})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[(\mathbb{G}^{k+1}_{k})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]

we have

\|(\mathcal{G}^{\text{even}}_{1})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\|_{\tilde{q}}\leq n\|(\mathbb{G}^{1}_{0})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\|_{\tilde{q}}+n\|(\mathbb{G}^{1}_{0})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\|_{1}.

But since

|(\mathbb{G}^{1}_{0})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}|\leq C^{\star}\left(1+\sum_{m}S^{m}([-A,1])+\sum_{p}\displaystyle\int_{-A}^{1}|W^{p}_{k}|d{D}^{p}_{k}\right)^{2}

it is clear that
(\mathbb{G}^{1}_{0})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}
has moments of any order
\tilde{q}\geq 1
bounded by a constant depending on both
\tilde{q}
and
\mathfrak{M}^{\star}
since its square root has exponential moments by Corollary
5.5
. Thus we can apply Lemma
12.3
with

M(q,\tau)\leq C^{\star}_{q}\tau n^{q},

and

\sigma_{n}^{2}=M(2,\tau)\leq C^{\star}\tau n^{2},

which concludes the proof.
∎

The next result controls the third term. As explain before, it is based on the fact the coupling is closed to the stationary process in
L^{2}
.

Proposition 12.4
.

There exist constants
C^{\star}
and
c^{\star}
such that

\big|\mathds{E}\left[(G_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]\big|\leq C^{\star}Te^{-c^{\star}n}.

It is sufficient to prove that

\big|\mathds{E}\left[(G_{0}^{1})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{1})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]\big|\leq C^{\star}e^{-c^{\star}n}.

As in the proof of Proposition
12.1
, for any
s\in[0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,X)\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,X)-\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,\mathbb{X})\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,\mathbb{X})|\leq\

\displaystyle|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,X)|\times|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,X)-\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,\mathbb{X})|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi^{\prime},s,\mathbb{X})|\times|\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,X)-\Psi^{\alpha}(\varphi,s,\mathbb{X})|

\displaystyle\leq C^{\star}(1+Y_{1}+\mathbb{Y}_{1})\Big(\sum_{m}

\displaystyle|(S^{m}\vartriangle\mathbb{S}^{m})([-A,1))|+\sum_{p}\displaystyle\int_{-A}^{1-}|W^{p}_{k}-\mathbb{W}^{p}_{k}|d{D}^{p}_{k}\Big).

Thus we have

|(G_{0}^{1})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-(\mathbb{G}_{0}^{1})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}|\leq C^{\star}(1+Y_{1}+\mathbb{Y}_{1})\Big(\sum_{m}(S^{m}\vartriangle\mathbb{S}^{m})([-A,1))+\sum_{p}\displaystyle\int_{-A}^{1-}|W^{p}_{k}-\mathbb{W}^{p}_{k}|d{D}^{p}_{k}\Big).

Clearly
(Y_{1}+\mathbb{Y}_{1})\in L^{2}
since it even has exponential moments: indeed
X\big|_{[-A,1)}
and
\mathbb{X}\big|_{[-A,1)}
have exponential moments. Moreover, since Corollary
6.4
applies and since
\ell\geq n/2
, we have, up to lowering
c^{\star}
,

\mathds{E}\left[\Big(\sum_{m}(S^{m}\vartriangle\mathbb{S}^{m})([-A,1))+\sum_{p}\displaystyle\int_{-A}^{1-}|W^{p}_{k}-\mathbb{W}^{p}_{k}|d{D}^{p}_{k}\Big)^{2}\right]\leq C^{\star}e^{-c^{\star}\ell}\leq C^{\star}e^{-c^{\star}n}.

Cauchy Schwarz inequality concludes.
∎

Finally we finish with the fourth term.

Proposition 12.5
.

For all
q\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C^{\star}_{q}
such that for all
x>0
,

\mathds{P}\Big(\big|(G^{T}_{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[(G^{T}_{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]\big|\geq x\Big)\leq\frac{C^{\star}_{q}r^{q}}{x^{q}}\leq\frac{C^{\star}_{q}n^{q}}{x^{q}}.

As in the proof of Proposition
12.4
, it is clear that
(G^{T}_{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}
has moments of any order bounded by
C^{\star}_{q}(T-2n\tau)^{q}=C^{\star}_{q}r^{q}\leq C^{\star}_{q}n^{q}
. Thus we have that

\mathds{E}\left[\big|(G^{T}_{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[(G^{T}_{2n\tau})_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]\big|^{q}\right]\leq C^{\star}_{q}n^{q}.

Markov inequality allows to conclude.
∎

Let us now combine all previous controls. We apply the second result of Proposition
12.1
, Proposition
12.2
with
Tx/2
instead of
x
, Proposition
12.4
, and Proposition
12.5
with
Tx/2
instead of
x
. Thus, for each
\varphi
, we have that

\big|G_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[G_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]\big|\leq C^{\star}Te^{-c^{\star}n}+Tx/2+C^{\star}Te^{-c^{\star}n}+Tx/2=Tx+C^{\star}Te^{-c^{\star}n}

with probability at least

1-C^{\star}\tau e^{-c^{\star}n}-\frac{C^{\star}_{q}\tau n^{q}}{T^{q}x^{q}}-4\exp\Big(-\frac{c^{\star}_{q}T^{2}x^{2}}{\tau n^{2}}\Big)-\frac{C^{\star}_{q}n^{q}}{T^{q}x^{q}}.

First since
T\geq 2n\tau
, we have that
\frac{C^{\star}_{q}\tau n^{q}}{T^{q}x^{q}}\leq\frac{C^{\star}_{q}}{\tau^{q-1}x^{q}}
. Then we also have

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{c^{\star}_{q}T^{2}x^{2}}{\tau n^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-c^{\star}_{q}\tau x^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\star}_{q}}{\tau^{q-1}x^{2(q-1)}}

where we used the fact that
y^{s}e^{-cy}\leq(s/ec)^{s}
for
y,c,s>0
. Finally, we have that
\frac{C^{\star}_{q}n^{q}}{T^{q}x^{q}}\leq\frac{C^{\star}_{q}}{\tau^{q}x^{q}}\leq\frac{C^{\star}_{q}}{\tau^{q-1}x^{q}}
which concludes the proof of Lemma
7.12
.

12.3
Proof of Theorem
7.14
and Proposition
7.15

The proof of Theorem
7.14
is based on Lemma
7.12
and Proposition
7.11
and consists in gathering all previous bounds.

Let us start by defining
n,\tau
and
r
to apply Lemma
7.12
. Let

n=\left\lfloor\Big(\frac{T}{2}\Big)^{1/2}\right\rfloor,\quad\tau=\left\lfloor\frac{T}{2n}\right\rfloor,\quad r=T-2n\tau.

Since
T\geq 8(A+1)^{2}
we have,
n\geq 2A+1
and since
T\geq 2
we have
n\leq\sqrt{T/2}\leq T/2
ensuring
\tau\geq 1
. We also have,

n=\left\lfloor(T/2)^{1/2}\right\rfloor\geq\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{T}{2}\Big)^{1/2}

and

\tau=\left\lfloor\frac{T}{2n}\right\rfloor\geq\left\lfloor\frac{T}{2(T/2)^{1/2}}\right\rfloor\geq\left\lfloor\Big(\frac{T}{2}\Big)^{1/2}\right\rfloor\geq\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{T}{2}\Big)^{1/2}.

We also have
n\leq\big(\frac{T}{2}\big)^{1/2}
and
\tau\leq(2T)^{1/2}
. Let us now apply Lemma
7.12
for each pair
(\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})\in\bm{\Phi}^{2}
with
x=\frac{\alpha\eta_{0}}{2s(c+1)^{2}}
. We obtain that,

\forall\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}\in\bm{\Phi},\quad\big|G_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[G_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]\big|\leq Tx+TC^{\star}e^{-c^{\star}n}

(12.8)

with probability at least

1-\card(\bm{\Phi})^{2}C^{\star}\tau e^{-c^{\star}n}-\card(\bm{\Phi})^{2}\frac{C^{\star}_{q}}{\tau^{q-1}}\big(\frac{1}{x^{q}}+\frac{1}{x^{2(q-1)}}\big).

(12.9)

Since
T^{1/2}\geq C^{\star}\log^{2}\big(C^{\star}(c+1)^{2}s/(\alpha\eta_{0})\big)
, we have that
C^{\star}e^{-c^{\star}n}\leq x
and thus (
12.8
) rewrites as follows,

\big|G_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[G_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]\big|\leq 2Tx=T\frac{\alpha\eta_{0}}{s(c+1)^{2}}.

Let us look at
\tau e^{-c^{\star}n}
. We have

\displaystyle\tau e^{-c^{\star}n}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{1/2}T^{1/2}e^{-(c^{\star}/2\sqrt{2})T^{1/2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T^{(q-1)/2}}2^{1/2}T^{q/2}e^{-(c^{\star}/2\sqrt{2})T^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\star}_{q}}{T^{(q-1)/2}}

where we used the same argument
y^{s}e^{-cy}\leq(s/ec)^{s}
for
y,c,s>0
as at the end of the proof of Lemma
7.12
. Since
q\leq 2(q-1)
we have

\frac{1}{x^{q}}+\frac{1}{x^{2(q-1)}}\leq 2\Big(1+\frac{1}{x^{2(q-1)}}\Big),

(12.10)

and (
12.9
) rewrites

1-\card(\bm{\Phi})^{2}\frac{C^{\star}_{q}}{T^{(q-1)/2}}\big(1+\frac{1}{x^{2(q-1)}}\big).

The change of variable
q\leftarrow(q-1)/2
leads to, for any
q>1/2
,

\mathds{P}\left(\forall\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}\in\bm{\Phi},\ \big|G_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}-\mathds{E}\left[G_{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}\right]\big|\leq T\frac{\alpha\eta_{0}}{s(c+1)^{2}}\right)\geq 1-\card(\bm{\Phi})^{2}\frac{C^{\star}_{q}}{T^{q}}\Big(1+\Big(\frac{s(c+1)^{2}}{\alpha\eta_{0}}\Big)^{4q}\Big).

Finally, Proposition
7.11
concludes the proof.
∎

Let us finish with the proof of Proposition
7.15

Recall that
X
is the 1-stationary process
\bm{{\HAR}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\star}}\bm{(}-\infty\bm{)}
from Theorem
3.9
. Let
\mathds{Q}
the distribution under which on
(-\infty,-A)
the process
X
has the same distribution as under
\mathds{P}
, and such that, on
[-A,\infty)
the process
X
is independent of the process on
(-\infty,-A)
and distributed as follows,

•

for
m\in\bm{M}
, the point processes
S^{m}\cap[-A,\infty)
are independent Poisson processes of rate
1
,

•

For all
(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}
with
k\geq-A
, the chains
W^{p}_{k}
are independent centered gaussian random variables with variance
\sigma_{p}^{2}
, also independent of the point processes.

The likelihood
\frac{d\mathds{P}}{d\mathds{Q}}
up to time
1
writes as follows,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}=\exp\left(M(1+A)-\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{-A}^{1}\lambda^{m}_{s}ds+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{-A}^{1}\log(\lambda^{m}_{s})dS^{m}_{s}+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\frac{1}{2\sigma_{p}^{2}}\displaystyle\int_{-A}^{1}(W^{p}_{k})^{2}-(W^{p}_{k}-\bar{W}^{p}_{k})^{2}d{D}^{p}_{k}\right)

where
\bar{W}^{p}_{k}=\Psi^{p}(\mathfrak{N}^{\star},t,X_{k-A,k})\underset{\text{under}\ \mathds{P}}{=}W^{p}_{k}-\varepsilon^{p}_{k}
. Define the following event,

\Omega_{B}=\bigcap_{m\in\bm{M}}\big(S^{m}([-2A,1])\leq B\big)\ \cap\bigcap_{p\in\bm{P}}\big(\displaystyle\int_{-2A}^{1}|W^{p}_{k}|d{D}^{p}_{k}\leq B\big).

Under the distribution
\mathds{Q}
it is clear that there exist constants
C^{\star},c^{\star}
such that
\mathds{P}_{\mathds{Q}}(\Omega_{B})\geq 1-C^{\star}e^{-c^{\star}B}
. Indeed, on the interval
[-2A,-A)
we have exponential moment by Corollary
5.5
and on
[-A,1]
we have independent Poisson processes of rate
1
and independent gaussian variables. Since the interaction functions are bounded and since
\min_{m\in\bm{M}}\mu_{m}>0
it is clear that on the event
\Omega_{B}
we have

\mathcal{L}_{1}\geq e^{-C^{\star}B^{2}}\quad\text{on}\ \Omega_{B},

with
C^{\star}
a constant depending only on
\mathfrak{M}
. Let
x
such that
|x_{J^{c}}|_{1}\leq c|x_{J}|
and without loss of generality suppose that
|x|_{2}=1
. Then since
x\cdot G^{1}_{0}x\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\left[x\cdot G_{0}^{1}x\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}_{\mathds{Q}}[x\cdot G_{0}^{1}x\times\mathcal{L}_{1}]

\displaystyle\geq\mathds{E}_{\mathds{Q}}[x\cdot G_{0}^{1}x\times\mathcal{L}_{1}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{B}}]

\displaystyle\geq C^{\star}e^{-C^{\star}B^{2}}\mathds{E}_{\mathds{Q}}[x\cdot G_{0}^{1}x\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{B}}]

\displaystyle=C^{\star}e^{-C^{\star}B^{2}}\Big(x\cdot\mathds{E}_{\mathds{Q}}[G_{0}^{1}]x-x\cdot\mathds{E}_{\mathds{Q}}[G_{0}^{1}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{B}^{c}}]x\Big)

\displaystyle\geq C^{\star}e^{-C^{\star}B^{2}}\Big(\eta-\|\mathds{E}_{\mathds{Q}}[G_{0}^{1}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{B}^{c}}]\|_{\infty}|x|_{1}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq C^{\star}e^{-C^{\star}B^{2}}\Big(\eta-s(c+1)^{2}\max_{\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}}\mathds{E}_{\mathds{Q}}[(G_{0}^{1})_{\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}}^{2}]^{1/2}\mathds{P}_{\mathds{Q}}(\Omega_{B}^{c})^{1/2}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq C^{\star}e^{-C^{\star}B^{2}}\Big(\eta-C^{\star}s(c+1)^{2}e^{-c^{\star}B}\Big)

where we used Cauchy Schwarz inequality to pass from line 5 to line 6. To conclude the proof one can remark that
\mathds{E}\left[G\right]=T\mathds{E}\left[G_{0}^{1}\right]
.
∎

A
Well posedness and stochastic intensity

In this appendix we prove that
d\pi-dtdx
is a
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t}
martingale even if
\mathcal{F}_{t}
is bigger than the natural history of
\pi
since it also contains information about the random drifts
\xi
.

Let
(\Omega_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1},P_{1})
a probability space,
\pi
a Poisson random measure on
\mathds{R}^{2}
on this space and the natural filtration

\mathcal{F}^{1}_{t}=\sigma\big(\pi\cap(-\infty,t]\times\mathds{R})\big).

Let
(\Omega_{2},\mathcal{A}_{2},P_{2})
another probability space and
(Z_{t})_{t\in\mathds{R}}
a process (the random drifts
\xi
in HAR framework) and denote by
\mathcal{F}^{2}
the natural history of
Z
. Consider
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathds{P})
the probability space defined by

\Omega=\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2},\quad\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{A}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{A}_{2},\quad\text{and}\ \mathds{P}=P_{1}\otimes P_{2}.

Then
\pi
can be viewed as a random variable on
\Omega
by
\pi(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=\pi(\omega_{1})
for any
(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\in\Omega
, and similarly for
Z
. In
\Omega
it is clear that
\pi
and
Z
are independent. Consider the filtration

\mathcal{F}_{t}=\sigma\Big((Z_{s})_{s\leq t};\ \pi\cap(-\infty,t]\times\mathds{R}\Big).

It is clear that we have
\mathcal{F}_{t}=\mathcal{F}^{1}_{t}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{2}_{t}
.

Denote by
\Pi
the predictable
\sigma
-algebra for
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t}
, i.e. the
\sigma
-algebra generated by sets of the form
(s,t]\times B\times A
with
s<t
,
B
a borelian set or
\mathds{R}
and
A\in\mathcal{F}_{s}
. Similarly, let
\Pi^{1}
the predictable
\sigma
-algebra for
(\mathcal{F}^{1}_{t})_{t}
.

Let

X:(t,x,\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\in\mathds{R}\times\mathds{R}_{+}\times\Omega\longmapsto X_{t}(x,\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\in\mathds{R}

be a
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t}
predictable process, which means that
X
is
\Pi
measurable. We want to show that for any
\omega_{2}\in\Omega_{2}
, the process

X^{\omega_{2}}:(t,x,\omega_{1})\in\mathds{R}\times\mathds{R}_{+}\times\Omega_{1}\longmapsto X_{t}(x,\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\in\mathds{R}

is
(\mathcal{F}^{1}_{t})_{t}
predictable, ie
\Pi^{1}
measurable.

Denote by
\mathcal{G}
the class of processes which satisfy the above property. Clearly for any
s<t
,
B
borelian,
A_{1}\in\mathcal{F}^{1}_{s}
and
A_{2}\in\mathcal{F}^{2}_{s}
, the following process is in
\mathcal{G}
,

\mathds{1}_{(s,t]\times B\times A_{1}\times A_{2}}.

Since
\mathcal{G}
is stable by linear combinations and by limits, and because
\mathcal{F}_{t}=\mathcal{F}^{1}_{t}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{2}_{t}
, it is clear that all the processes of the form

\mathds{1}_{(s,t]\times B\times A},

with
s<t
,
B
borelian,
A\in\mathcal{F}_{s}
are in
\mathcal{G}
. Thus for any
P\in\Pi
, we have
\mathds{1}_{P}\in\mathcal{G}
. Then it is clear that
\mathcal{G}
is exactly the
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t}
predictable processes. We proved that for all
\omega_{2}\in\Omega_{2}
, the process
X^{\omega_{2}}
is
(\mathcal{F}^{1}_{t})_{t}
predictable.

Thus, if
X
is non negative, by Fubini Theorem we have

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\left[\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}^{2}}X(t,x)d\pi(t,x)\right]

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}^{2}}X(t,x,\omega)d\pi(t,x)d\mathds{P}(\omega)

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{2}}\bigg[\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}^{2}}X^{\omega_{2}}(t,x,\omega_{1})d\pi(t,x)dP_{1}(\omega_{1})\bigg]dP_{2}(\omega_{2})

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{2}}\bigg[\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}^{2}}X^{\omega_{2}}(t,x,\omega_{1})dtdx\ dP_{1}(\omega_{1})\bigg]dP_{2}(\omega_{2})

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}^{2}}X(t,x,\omega)dtdx\ d\mathds{P}(\omega)

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}\left[\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}^{2}}X(t,x)dtdx\right].

Thus by taking
X(t,x)=\mathds{1}_{0\leq x\leq\lambda_{t}}
with
\lambda
a
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t}
predictable process, it is clear that the
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t}
compensator of

t\longmapsto\pi\big(\{t\}\times[0,\lambda_{t}]\big)\quad\text{is}\quad\lambda_{t}dt.

With this result in hand, we can prove that point processes of HAR processes are well defined if the intensity is defined
d\mathds{P}\left(\omega\right)\otimes dt
almost everywhere.

Let
\lambda
and
\Upsilon
two predictable processes, which stand for the intensity in HAR framework. Denote
S_{\lambda}
and
S_{\Upsilon}
the points under respectively
\lambda
and
\Upsilon
. It means that

S_{\lambda}=\{s\in\mathds{R}\mid\exists 0\leq x\leq\lambda_{s},\ (s,x)\in\pi\}.

Suppose that
d\mathds{P}\left(\omega\right)\otimes dt
a.e. we have
\lambda=\Upsilon
. Then we have
S_{\lambda}=S_{\Upsilon}
almost surely. Indeed,

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\left[\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}\times\mathds{R}_{+}}\mathds{1}_{\min(\lambda_{s},\Upsilon_{s})<x\leq\max(\lambda_{s},\Upsilon_{s})}d\pi(s,x)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}\left[\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}\times\mathds{R}_{+}}\mathds{1}_{\min(\lambda_{s},\Upsilon_{s})<x\leq\max(\lambda_{s},\Upsilon_{s})}dsdx\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathds{E}\left[\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}\times\mathds{R}_{+}}\mathds{1}_{\lambda_{s}\neq\Upsilon_{s}}dsdx\right]

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}\times\mathds{R}_{+}}\left[\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\mathds{R}}\mathds{1}_{\lambda_{s}\neq\Upsilon_{s}}d\mathds{P}\left(\omega\right)\otimes ds\right]dx

\displaystyle=0.

Thus it follows that
S_{\lambda}=S_{\Upsilon}
almost surely.

B
Linear HAR processes

In this appendix we present two additional results useful to the study of linear HAR processes. First, in Lemma
B.1
, we derive
L^{1}
bounds and analytic properties of the coefficients defined in Lemma
4.3
. Then we extend the use of the coefficients to random sequences in Proposition
B.2
.

Lemma B.1
.

Let some parameters
\mathfrak{M}\in\mathfrak{L}
. Then the coefficients
\left(\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}\right)_{(p,k),(p^{\prime},k^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Q},\ k\geq k^{\prime}}
from Lemma
4.3
satisfy the following properties.

1.

If assumption
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec1.1}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
holds, then for any
(p_{0},k_{0})\in\mathcal{Q}
for any
p\in\bm{P}
we have,

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k_{0}}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p_0,k_0}{p,k}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p}_{k}\leq\Big[(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}\Big]_{p_{0},p}.

2.

If assumption
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec1.1}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
holds, then for any
p_{0},p\in\bm{P}
we have,

\sum_{k_{0}\in\mathcal{D}_{p_{0}}\cap[0,1)}\displaystyle\int_{k_{0}}^{\infty}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p_0,k_0}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p}_{k}\leq n_{p}\times\Big[(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}\Big]_{p,p_{0}}.

3.

For non negative sequence
(Z^{p}_{k})_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}}
, the sequence
V
defined by

V^{p}_{k}=\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}Z^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}},

satisfies

V^{p}_{k}=Z^{p}_{k}+\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k-}a^{p}_{p^{\prime}}h^{p}_{p^{\prime}}(k-k^{\prime})V^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}.

The same is true for sequences
(Z^{p}_{k})_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}}
such that for all
(p_{0},k_{0})\in\mathcal{Q}
the following series is absolutely convergent,

\sum_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q},\ k<k_{0}}\left[\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{k}^{k_{0}-}a^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}h^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}(k_{0}-k^{\prime})\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p',k'}{p,k}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}Z^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}\right]

which is the case if
Z
is bounded and if assumption
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec1.1}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
holds.

Point 1 and point 2 give bounds on, respectively, the ascending and descending sum of the coefficients in term of the matrix
H^{W}_{W}
. Point 3 extends the use of the coefficients to sequences infinite on both sides.

The following result extends the use of coefficients to random sequences and also deals with uniqueness.

Proposition B.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{M}\in\mathfrak{L}
and suppose that
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec1.1}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
holds. Suppose that
V
is a sequence of random variable such that
\sup_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathds{E}\left[|V^{p}_{k}|\right]<\infty
and satisfying

V^{p}_{k}=\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k-}a^{p}_{p^{\prime}}h^{p}_{p^{\prime}}(k-k^{\prime})V^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}.

Then, we must have
V^{p}_{k}=0
for all
(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}
. Therefore, for any sequence
Z
with
\sup_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathds{E}\left[|Z^{p}_{k}|\right]<\infty
, the equation

V^{p}_{k}=Z^{p}_{k}+\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k-}a^{p}_{p^{\prime}}h^{p}_{p^{\prime}}(k-k^{\prime})V^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}

has a unique solution among sequences
V
for which
\sup_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathds{E}\left[|V^{p}_{k}|\right]<\infty
, being

V^{p}_{k}=\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}Z^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}.

We start with
point 1
.
Let for
p_{0},p\in\bm{P}
and
\tau\geq 1
,

N_{p_{0},p}(\tau):=\sup_{k_{0}\in\mathcal{D}_{p_{0}}}\displaystyle\int_{k_{0}-\tau}^{k_{0}}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p_0,k_0}{p,k}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p}_{k}.

Let
k_{0}\in\mathcal{D}_{p_{0}}
, then by point 3 of Lemma
4.3
we have,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{k_{0}-\tau}^{k_{0}}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p_0,k_0}{p,k}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{p=p_{0}}+\displaystyle\int_{k_{0}-\tau}^{k_{0}-}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p_0,k_0}{p,k}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{p=p_{0}}+\displaystyle\int_{k_{0}-\tau}^{k_{0}-}\left[\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{k}^{k_{0}-}a^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}h^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}(k_{0}-k^{\prime})\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p',k'}{p,k}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}\right]d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{p=p_{0}}+\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{k_{0}-\tau}^{k_{0}-}\displaystyle\int_{k_{0}-\tau}^{k^{\prime}}a^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}h^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}(k_{0}-k^{\prime})\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p',k'}{p,k}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathds{1}_{p=p_{0}}+\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{k_{0}-\tau}^{k_{0}-}a^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}h^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}(k_{0}-k^{\prime})N_{p^{\prime},p}(\tau)d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathds{1}_{p=p_{0}}+\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}a^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}\|h^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}\|_{1,n_{p^{\prime}}}^{\infty}N_{p^{\prime},p}(\tau).

Taking the supremum on
k_{0}
gives

N_{p_{0},p}(\tau)\leq\mathds{1}_{p=p_{0}}+\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}a^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}\|h^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}\|_{1,n_{p^{\prime}}}^{\infty}N_{p^{\prime},p}(\tau).

Thus if
N(\tau)=(N_{p,p^{\prime}}(\tau))_{p,p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}
we have (one should recall that
L_{p}=1
)

N(\tau)\preceq\Id+H^{W}_{W}N(\tau).

Equivalently,

(\Id-H^{W}_{W})N(\tau)\preceq\Id.

Since all the entries of
(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}=\Id+H^{W}_{W}+(H^{W}_{W})^{2}+\cdots
are non negative, one can multiply both sides by
(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}
leading to

N(\tau)\preceq(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1},

and so

N_{p_{0},p}(\tau)\leq\Big[(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}\Big]_{p_{0},p}.

Letting
\tau\to\infty
gives the intended result.

Point 2
is a consequence of
point 1
and 1-periodicity. Indeed,

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{0}\in\mathcal{D}_{p_{0}}\cap[0,1)}\displaystyle\int_{k_{0}}^{\infty}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p_0,k_0}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{0}\in\mathcal{D}_{p_{0}}\cap[0,1)}\displaystyle\int_{k_{0}}^{\infty}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k-\floor{k}}{p_0,k_0-\floor{k}}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathcal{D}_{p}\cap[0,1)}\sum_{k_{0}\in\mathcal{D}_{p_{0}}\cap[0,1)}\displaystyle\int_{k_{0}}^{\infty}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k_1}{p_0,k_0-\floor{k}}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}\mathds{1}_{k-\lfloor k\rfloor=k_{1}}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathcal{D}_{p}\cap[0,1)}\sum_{k_{0}\in\mathcal{D}_{p_{0}}\cap[0,1)}\sum_{\mathds{Z}\ni j\geq k_{0}-k_{1}}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k_1}{p_0,k_0-j}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathcal{D}_{p}\cap[0,1)}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k_{1}}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k_1}{p_0,k}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p_{0}}_{k}.

Here
point 1
concludes the proof of
point 2
since there
\card(\mathcal{D}_{p}\cap[0,1))=n_{p}
.
Let us tackle
point 3
. From point 3 of Lemma
4.3
we have (the assumptions allow us to use Fubini Theorem),

\displaystyle V^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}Z^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=Z^{p}_{k}+\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k-}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}Z^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=Z^{p}_{k}+\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k-}\left[\sum_{p^{\prime\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{k^{\prime}}^{k-}a^{p}_{p^{\prime\prime}}h^{p}_{p^{\prime\prime}}(k-k^{\prime\prime})\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p'',k''}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p^{\prime\prime}}_{k^{\prime\prime}}\right]Z^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=Z^{p}_{k}+\sum_{p^{\prime\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k-}a^{p}_{p^{\prime\prime}}h^{p}_{p^{\prime\prime}}(k-k^{\prime\prime})\left[\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k^{\prime\prime}}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p'',k''}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}Z^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}\right]d{D}^{p^{\prime\prime}}_{k^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle=Z^{p}_{k}+\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k-}a^{p}_{p^{\prime}}h^{p}_{p^{\prime}}(k-k^{\prime})V^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}.

Which is the result. Finally, if
Z
is bounded, say by
Z_{\infty}<\infty
and
\spr(H^{W}_{W})<1
then,

\displaystyle\sum_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q},\ k<k_{0}}\left[\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{k}^{k_{0}-}a^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}h^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}(k_{0}-k^{\prime})\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p',k'}{p,k}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}Z^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq Z_{\infty}\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k_{0}-}\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{k}^{k_{0}-}a^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}h^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}(k_{0}-k^{\prime})\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p',k'}{p,k}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle=Z_{\infty}\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k_{0}-}\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k^{\prime}}a^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}h^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}(k_{0}-k^{\prime})\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p',k'}{p,k}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}d{D}^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq Z_{\infty}\max_{p^{\prime}}\bigg(\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\Big[(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}\Big]_{p^{\prime},p}\bigg)\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k_{0}-}a^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}h^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}(k_{0}-k^{\prime})d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq Z_{\infty}\max_{p^{\prime}}\bigg(\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\Big[(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}\Big]_{p^{\prime},p}\bigg)\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}a^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}\|h^{p_{0}}_{p^{\prime}}\|_{1,n_{p^{\prime}}}^{\infty}<\infty.

Where we use
point 1
between the third and the fourth line.
∎

Let
{V}^{p}_{\infty}=\sup_{k\in\mathcal{D}_{p}}\mathds{E}\left[|V^{p}_{k}|\right]
for
p\in\bm{P}
. Then for any
p\in\bm{P}
we have,

\mathds{E}\left[|V^{p}_{k}|\right]\leq\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k-}a^{p}_{p^{\prime}}h^{p}_{p^{\prime}}(k-k^{\prime})\mathds{E}\left[|V^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}|\right]d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}\leq\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}(H^{W}_{W})_{pp^{\prime}}{V}^{p^{\prime}}_{\infty}.

Thus, by taking a supremum on
k\in\mathcal{D}_{p}
,

{V}^{p}_{\infty}\leq\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}(H^{W}_{W})_{pp^{\prime}}{V}^{p^{\prime}}_{\infty}=(H^{W}_{W}{V}_{\infty})_{p}.

Iterating this formula gives
{V}_{\infty}\preceq(H^{W}_{W})^{n}{V}_{\infty}\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\bm{0}_{\bm{P}}
which implies
{V}_{\infty}=\bm{0}_{\bm{P}}
. Which proves the first result.

Finally, since
Z
is bounded in
L^{1}
the series in point 3 of Lemma
B.1
is absolutely convergent in expectation and thus it is almost surely absolutely convergent. It means that the random sequence
Y
is a solution of the equation and it is bounded in
L^{1}
since

\sup_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathds{E}\left[\left|\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k-}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}}Z^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}\right|\right]\leq{Z}_{\infty}\max_{p\in\bm{P}}\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\Big[(\Id-H^{W}_{W})^{-1}\Big]_{p,p^{\prime}}<\infty

where
{Z}_{\infty}=\sup_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathds{E}\left[|Z^{p}_{k}|\right]
.
∎

C
Exponential moments

In this appendix we state and prove the key result to derive exponential moments for HAR processes. Lemma
C.1
states exponential moments for linear combinations of both the random drifts and point processes with clusters generated by the cluster functions defined in Definition
4.7
and immigrant rates depending on linear functionals of the random drifts. Recall that the random drifts
(\xi^{p}_{k})_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}}
are independent random real variables such that for all
(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}
we have
\xi^{p}_{k}\sim\xi^{p}_{k+n}
for any
n\in\mathds{Z}
.

Consider the random variable

\mathcal{Z}=\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}B^{m}(t)dS^{m}_{t}+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}C^{p}_{k}\xi^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}=:B\bullet S+C\bullet\xi,

where we have

•

(S^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}}
a multivariate point process such that conditionally to the
(\xi^{p}_{k})_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}}
it has immigrant rates being

\mathcal{I}^{m}_{t}={i}_{m}+\sum_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}}A^{m}_{p,k}(t)\xi^{p}_{k}

and independent clusters generated by the bivariate functions
(\mathfrak{h}^{m}_{m^{\prime}})_{m,m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}
. In other words, the intensity
\lambda^{m}_{t}
of
S^{m}
at time
t
is

\lambda^{m}_{t}=\mathcal{I}^{m}_{t}+\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t-}\mathfrak{h}^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(s,t)dS^{m^{\prime}}_{s}.

•

{i}:=({i}_{m})_{m\in\bm{M}}
is a non negative vector.

•

A^{m}_{p,k}
is a non negative real function for all
m\in\bm{M}
,
(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}
. We also define the following matrix

A_{\infty}=\Big(\sup_{t\in\mathds{R}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}A^{m}_{p,k}(t)d{D}^{p}_{k}\Big)_{(p,m)\in\bm{P}\times\bm{M}}.

•

B=(B^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}}
is a vector of non negative real functions and for a positive integer
n
we define the vector

B_{\infty,n}=\Big(\|B^{m}\|^{\infty}_{1,1/n}\Big)_{m\in\bm{M}}.

•

C=(C^{p})_{p\in\bm{P}}
is a vector of non negative functions on the
\mathcal{D}_{p}
’s and we define the vector

C_{\infty}=\Big(\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}C^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}\Big)_{p\in\bm{P}}.

We can now state the main result.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
n\geq 1
an integer. Suppose that assumption
\bm{(}\text{{{SubG}}}\bm{)}
[\xi]
holds. Then we have

\mathds{E}\left[e^{\mathcal{Z}}\right]\leq\exp\Big[{i}^{\intercal}(ne^{\bm{\bm{{L}}}(B_{\infty,n})}-n\bm{1})+\mathfrak{e}^{\intercal}\bm{\mathcal{X}}+\bm{\mathcal{X}}^{\intercal}\diag(\mathfrak{s})\bm{\mathcal{X}}\Big]

where

\bm{\mathcal{X}}=C_{\infty}+A_{\infty}(ne^{\bm{\bm{{L}}}(B_{\infty,n})}-n\bm{1}),

with
\bm{{L}}
the log-Laplace function of a
{P}\!{o}\!{i}\!{s}(\mathfrak{H}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\intercal})
Galton Watson tree defined in Theorem 3.1 of
[
41
]
.

By conditioning on the random drifts we obtain

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\left[e^{\mathcal{Z}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}\left[\mathds{E}\left[e^{\mathcal{Z}}\ \middle|\ \xi\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathds{E}\left[e^{C\bullet\xi}\mathds{E}\left[e^{B\bullet S}\ \middle|\ \xi\right]\right]

Let us investigate the term
\mathds{E}\left[e^{B\bullet S}\ \middle|\ \xi\right]
. We will use the cluster decomposition. Let
G^{m}_{t}
a
{P}\!{o}\!{i}\!{s}(\bm{\mathfrak{h}}^{\intercal})
