born at time
t\in\mathds{R}
with root of type
m\in\bm{M}
and let

G^{m}_{t}(B):=\sum_{(u,m^{\prime},s)\in G^{m}_{t}}B^{m^{\prime}}(s)

the contribution of cluster
G^{m}_{t}
to
B\bullet S
. From the cluster decomposition of
S
we have

\mathds{E}\left[e^{B\bullet S}\ \middle|\ \xi\right]=\exp\Big[\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}(\psi^{m}(t,B)-1)\mathcal{I}^{m}_{t}dt\Big]

where

\psi^{m}(t,B)=\mathds{E}\left[e^{G^{m}_{t}(B)}\right].

We can now use the expression of the immigrant rates
\mathcal{I}^{m}
,

\mathds{E}\left[e^{B\bullet S}\ \middle|\ \xi\right]=\exp\Big[\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}{i}_{m}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}(\psi^{m}(t,B)-1)dt+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}\xi^{p}_{k}\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}A^{m}_{p,k}(t)(\psi^{m}(t,B)-1)dtd{D}^{p}_{k}\Big]

Thus we have

\mathds{E}\left[e^{\mathcal{Z}}\right]=\exp\Big[\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}{i}_{m}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}(\psi^{m}(t,B)-1)dt\Big]\mathds{E}\left[\exp\Big(\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}D^{p}_{k}\xi^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}\Big)\right]

with

D^{p}_{k}=C^{p}_{k}+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}A^{m}_{p,k}(t)(\psi^{m}(t,B)-1)dt.

Thus from assumption
\bm{(}\text{{{SubG}}}\bm{)}
[\xi]
we have

\mathds{E}\left[e^{\mathcal{Z}}\right]\leq\exp\Big[\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}{i}_{m}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}(\psi^{m}(t,B)-1)dt+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\mathfrak{e}_{p}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}D^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\mathfrak{s}_{p}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}(D^{p}_{k})^{2}d{D}^{p}_{k}\Big]

Let us focus on
\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}D^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}
. We have,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}D^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}\Big(C^{p}_{k}+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}A^{m}_{p,k}(t)(\psi^{m}(t,B)-1)dt\Big)d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle=(C_{\infty})_{p}+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}A^{m}_{p,k}(t)d{D}^{p}_{k}(\psi^{m}(t,B)-1)dt

\displaystyle\leq(C_{\infty})_{p}+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}(A_{\infty})_{p,m}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}(\psi^{m}(t,B)-1)dt.

To continue the proof we have to bound
\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}(\psi^{m}(t,B)-1)dt
for
m\in\bm{M}
. Recall that
n
is a positive integer. We use the same ideas as in Theorem 3.10 of
[
41
]
except the we have to deal with
1
-stationary.

For
t\in\mathds{R}
, denote by
\lfloor t\rfloor_{n}
and
\{t\}_{n}
the unique real numbers such that
t=\lfloor t\rfloor_{n}+\{t\}_{n}
with
\lfloor t\rfloor_{n}\in n\mathds{Z}
,
0\leq\{t\}_{n}<n
. Thus we have by 1-periodicity of clusters, if
\tau_{x}(s)=x+s
, we have

\psi^{m}(t,B)=\mathds{E}\left[e^{G^{m}_{t}(B)}\right]=\psi^{m}(\{t\}_{n},B\circ\tau_{\lfloor t\rfloor_{n}}).

It follows that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}(\psi^{m}(t,B)-1)dt

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{n}\sum_{j\in\mathds{Z}}(\psi^{m}(t+nj,B)-1)dt

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{n}\sum_{j\in\mathds{Z}}(\psi^{m}(t,B\circ\tau_{nj})-1)dt

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{n}\mathds{E}\left[\sum_{j\in\mathds{Z}}(e^{G^{m}_{t}(B\circ\tau_{nj})}-1)\right]dt

\displaystyle\leq\displaystyle\int_{0}^{n}\mathds{E}\left[-1+\prod_{j\in\mathds{Z}}e^{G^{m}_{t}(B\circ\tau_{nj})}\right]dt

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{n}\mathds{E}\left[-1+e^{G^{m}_{t}(\sum_{j\in\mathds{Z}}B\circ\tau_{nj})}\right]dt

\displaystyle\leq\displaystyle\int_{0}^{n}\mathds{E}\left[-1+e^{G^{m}_{t}(B_{\infty,n})}\right]dt

\displaystyle\leq\displaystyle\int_{0}^{n}\big[e^{\bm{\bm{{L}}}(B_{\infty,n})}-\bm{1}\big]_{m}dt

\displaystyle=n\big[e^{\bm{\bm{{L}}}(B_{\infty,n})}-\bm{1}\big]_{m}.

The last inequality comes from the fact that
\{(u,m^{\prime})\mid(u,m^{\prime},s)\in G^{m}_{t}\}
is stochastically dominated by a
{P}\!{o}\!{i}\!{s}(\mathfrak{H}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\intercal})
Galton Watson process, and thus, with the notations of Theorem 3.1 of
[
41
]
,
G^{m}_{t}(B_{\infty,n})\leq B_{\infty,n}\cdot\card_{\bm{M}}(\mathcal{T}^{m})
for a well coupled
{P}\!{o}\!{i}\!{s}(\mathfrak{H}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\intercal})
Galton Watson process
\mathcal{T}^{m}
with root of type
m
.
We conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}D^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}\leq(C_{\infty})_{p}+\big[A_{\infty}(ne^{\bm{\bm{{L}}}(B_{\infty,n})}-n\bm{1})\big]_{p}.

Finally, since
\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}(D^{p}_{k})^{2}d{D}^{p}_{k}\leq\Big(\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}D^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}\Big)^{2}
we can conclude that

\displaystyle\mathds{E}\left[e^{\mathcal{Z}}\right]

\displaystyle=\exp\Big[{i}_{m}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}(\psi^{m}(t,B)-1)dt+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\mathfrak{e}_{p}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}D^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\mathfrak{s}_{p}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}(D^{p}_{k})^{2}d{D}^{p}_{k}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big[\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}{i}_{m}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}(\psi^{m}(t,B)-1)dt+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\mathfrak{e}_{p}\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}D^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\mathfrak{s}_{p}\Big(\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{R}}D^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big[{i}^{\intercal}(ne^{\bm{\bm{{L}}}(B_{\infty,n})}-n\bm{1})+\mathfrak{e}^{\intercal}\bm{\mathcal{X}}+\bm{\mathcal{X}}^{\intercal}\diag(\mathfrak{s})\bm{\mathcal{X}}\Big]

where we denoted
\bm{\mathcal{X}}:=C_{\infty}+A_{\infty}(ne^{\bm{\bm{{L}}}(B_{\infty,n})}-n\bm{1})
.
∎

D
Asymptotic limit

This appendix is dedicated to Lemma
D.1
, a technical Lemma used in the proof of Theorem
6.3
. Recall that in the proof of Theorem
6.3
we have two HAR processes with different initial conditions, indexed by
i=1,2
. The process
\Big((\Delta\lambda^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},(\Delta S^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},(\Delta W^{p})_{p\in\bm{P}}\Big)
correspond to the difference, in absolute value between these two processes.

We recall that, to avoid confusion, in this proof, we denote by
g
the interaction functions of
\mathfrak{M}^{+}
and
\mathfrak{g}
the cluster functions for
\mathfrak{M}^{+}
.

Lemma
D.1
bounds the difference process by a linear HAR process with vanishing base rates.

Lemma D.1
.

Suppose that assumptions
\bm{(}\text{{{Spec1.1}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
and
\bm{(}\text{{{ExpTail}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
hold. Then there exists an adapted process
\Big((\mathcal{L}^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},({S}^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},({W}^{p})_{p\in\bm{P}}\Big)
and an adapted Poisson random measures
{P}^{m}
for
m\in\bm{M}
such that it dominates
\Big((\Delta\lambda^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},(\Delta S^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},(\Delta W^{p})_{p\in\bm{P}}\Big)
, which means

•

for
m\in\bm{M},\ t\geq t_{0}
,
\Delta\lambda^{m}(t)\leq\mathcal{L}^{m}_{t}
,

•

for
m\in\bm{M},\ t\geq t_{0}
,
d\Delta S^{m}(t)\leq d{S}^{m}_{t}
,

•

for
(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q},\ k\geq t_{0}
,
\Delta W^{p}(k)\leq{W}^{p}_{k}
.

And we have for all
m\in\bm{M},\ t\geq t_{0},\ C\subset[t_{0},\infty),\ (p,k)\in\mathcal{Q}
with
k\geq t_{0}
,

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}^{m}_{t}=u_{0}e^{-c(t-t_{0})}+\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t-}\mathfrak{g}^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(s,t)d{S}^{m^{\prime}}_{s}\\
&{S}^{m}(C)=\displaystyle\int_{C\times\mathds{R}_{+}}\mathds{1}_{0<x\leq\mathcal{L}^{m}_{s}}d{P}^{m}(s,x)\\
&{W}^{p}_{k}=u_{0}e^{-c(k-t_{0})}+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{k-}Ke^{-c(k-s)}d{S}^{m}_{s}\end{aligned}\right.

where
u_{0}=K|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}
with
K,c,\delta>0
constants depending only on
\mathfrak{M}
.

Recall that for
i=1,2
we have for
t\geq t_{0}
,
C\subset\mathds{R}
and
k\geq t_{0}

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\lambda^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{i}}_{t}=\Phi^{m}\bigg(\mu_{m}+\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t-}h^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(t-s)dS^{m^{\prime},\mathfrak{C}_{i}}_{s}+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t-}h^{m}_{p}(t-k)\kappa^{m}_{p}(W^{p,\mathfrak{C}_{i}}_{k})d{D}^{p}_{k}\bigg)\\
&S^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{i}}(C)=\card(\mathfrak{C}^{m}\cap C)+\displaystyle\int_{C\times\mathds{R}_{+}}\mathds{1}_{x\leq\lambda^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{i}}_{s}}d\pi^{m}(s,x)\\
&W^{p,\mathfrak{C}_{i}}_{k}=\xi^{p}_{k}+\Phi^{p}\bigg(\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k-}h^{p}_{m}(k-s)dS^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{i}}_{s}+\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{k-}h^{p}_{p^{\prime}}(k-k^{\prime})\kappa^{p}_{p^{\prime}}(W^{p^{\prime},\mathfrak{C}_{i}}_{k^{\prime}})d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}\bigg).\end{aligned}\right.

Let
{P}^{m}
the Poisson random measure on
[t_{0},\infty)\times\mathds{R}_{+}
defined by

{P}^{m}(s,x)=\pi^{m}\Big(s,x+\min\big[\lambda^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{1}}_{s},\lambda^{m,\mathfrak{C}_{2}}_{s}\big]\Big).

Then it follows that for
C\subset[t_{0},\infty)
,

\Delta S^{m}(C)=\displaystyle\int_{C\times\mathds{R}_{+}}\mathds{1}_{x\leq\Delta\lambda^{m}(s)}d{P}^{m}(s,x).

(D.1)

Since
\Phi^{m}
is
L_{m}
Lipschitz and from triangular inequality,

\displaystyle\Delta\lambda^{m}(t)\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t-}g^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(t-s)d\Delta S^{m^{\prime}}(s)+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t-}g^{m}_{p}(t-k)a^{m}_{p}\Delta W^{p}(k)d{D}^{p}_{k}

\displaystyle+\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t_{0}-}g^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(t-s)d(\mathfrak{C}^{m^{\prime}}_{1}\vartriangle d\mathfrak{C}^{m^{\prime}}_{2})_{s}

\displaystyle+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t_{0}-}g^{m}_{p}(t-k)|\kappa^{m}_{p}(\mathfrak{C}^{p}_{1,k})-\kappa^{m}_{p}(\mathfrak{C}^{p}_{2,k})|d{D}^{p}_{k}

with
g^{\alpha}_{\beta}=L_{\alpha}|h^{\alpha}_{\beta}|
. From
\bm{(}\text{{{ExpTail}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
there exists a constant
c_{1}>0
such that
g^{\alpha}_{\beta}(x)\leq K_{1}e^{-c_{1}x}
thus we have

\displaystyle\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t_{0}-}g^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(t-s)d(\mathfrak{C}^{m^{\prime}}_{1}\vartriangle\mathfrak{C}^{m^{\prime}}_{2})_{s}

\displaystyle\leq K_{1}e^{-c_{1}(t-t_{0})}\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t_{0}-}e^{-c_{1}(t_{0}-s)}d(\mathfrak{C}^{m^{\prime}}_{1}\vartriangle\mathfrak{C}^{m^{\prime}}_{2})_{s}

\displaystyle\leq Ke^{-c(t-t_{0})}|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}

with
c=\delta=c_{1}>0
and
K
depending on
\mathfrak{M}
. The function
\psi
, defined in (
6.1
) is such that for any Lipschitz function
f
with the convention
f(\varnothing)=0
, for any
x,y\in\mathds{R}\cup\{\varnothing\}
we have

|f(x)-f(y)|\leq(\lip(f)+|f(0)|)\psi(x,y).

Thus we have

|\kappa^{m}_{p}(\mathfrak{C}^{p}_{1,k})-\kappa^{m}_{p}(\mathfrak{C}^{p}_{2,k})|\leq K_{2}\psi(\mathfrak{C}^{p}_{1,k},\mathfrak{C}^{p}_{2,k})

where
K_{2}
is a constant depending on the masks
\kappa^{m}_{p}
. It follows that

\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t_{0}-}g^{m}_{p}(t-k)|\kappa^{m}_{p}(\mathfrak{C}^{p}_{1,k})-\kappa^{m}_{p}(\mathfrak{C}^{p}_{2,k})|d{D}^{p}_{k}\leq Ke^{-c(t-t_{0})}|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}.

Thus, if
u_{0}=K|\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}|_{\delta}
, up to increasing
K
we have,

\Delta\lambda^{m}(t)\leq u_{0}e^{-c(t-t_{0})}+\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t-}g^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(t-s)d\Delta S^{m^{\prime}}(s)+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t-}g^{m}_{p}(t-k)a^{m}_{p}\Delta W^{p}(k)d{D}^{p}_{k}

(D.2)

Similarly,

\Delta W^{p}(k)\leq u_{0}e^{-c(k-t_{0})}+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{k-}g^{p}_{m}(k-s)d\Delta S^{m}(s)+\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{k-}g^{p}_{p^{\prime}}(k-k^{\prime})a^{p}_{p^{\prime}}\Delta W^{p^{\prime}}(k^{\prime})d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}.

(D.3)

Thus a process
\Big((\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},(\tilde{{S}}^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},(\tilde{{W}}^{p})_{p\in\bm{P}}\Big)
satisfying equality in equations (
D.1
), (
D.2
) and (
D.3
), ie

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{m}_{t}=u_{0}e^{-c(t-t_{0})}+\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t-}g^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(t-s)d\tilde{{S}}^{m^{\prime}}_{s}+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t-}g^{m}_{p}(t-k)a^{m}_{p}\tilde{{W}}^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}\\
&\tilde{{S}}^{m}(C)=\displaystyle\int_{C\times\mathds{R}_{+}}\mathds{1}_{x\leq\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{m}_{s}}d{P}^{m}(s,x)\\
&\tilde{{W}}^{p}_{k}=u_{0}e^{-c(k-t_{0})}+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{k-}g^{p}_{m}(k-s)d\tilde{{S}}^{m}_{s}+\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{k-}g^{p}_{p^{\prime}}(k-k^{\prime})a^{p}_{p^{\prime}}\tilde{{W}}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}\end{aligned}\right.

(D.4)

must dominate
\Big((\Delta\lambda^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},(\Delta S^{m})_{m\in\bm{M}},(\Delta W^{p})_{p\in\bm{P}}\Big)
. Equations (
D.4
) is in fact linear HAR equations with parameter from
\mathfrak{M}_{+}
with
b^{\alpha}_{p}=0
and vanishing base rates instead of constant base rates for the point processes and random drifts for the chains. Thus one can use the results on linear models. More precisely, we can express exactly the process with the cluster functions
\mathfrak{g}
and the coefficients
\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}^{+}}
as follows,

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{m}_{t}=u_{0}e^{-c(t-t_{0})}+\sum_{p,p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t-}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{k}u_{0}e^{-c(k^{\prime}-t_{0})}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}^{+}}g^{m}_{p}(t-k)a^{m}_{p}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}d{D}^{p}_{k}+\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t-}\mathfrak{g}^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(t-s)d\tilde{{S}}^{m^{\prime}}_{s}\\
&\tilde{{S}}^{m}(C)=\displaystyle\int_{C\times\mathds{R}_{+}}\mathds{1}_{x\leq\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{m}_{s}}d{P}^{m}(s,x)\\
&\tilde{{W}}^{p}_{k}=\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{k}u_{0}e^{-c(k^{\prime}-t_{0})}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}^{+}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}+\sum_{p^{\prime}\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{k}\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{k^{\prime}-}g^{p}_{m}(k^{\prime}-s)d\tilde{{S}}^{m}_{s}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{p,k}{p',k'}_{\!\!\mathfrak{M}^{+}}d{D}^{p^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime}}\end{aligned}\right..

Then one can use the exponential decay of the coefficients stated in Proposition
6.1
, and
\bm{(}\text{{{ExpTail}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}^{+}]
(trivially implied by
\bm{(}\text{{{ExpTail}}}\bm{)}
[\mathfrak{M}]
) to prove that we have (up to reducing
c
and increasing
K
),

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{m}_{t}\leq u_{0}Ke^{-c(t-t_{0})}+\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t-}\mathfrak{g}^{m}_{m^{\prime}}(t-s)d\tilde{{S}}^{m^{\prime}}_{s}\\
&\tilde{{S}}^{m}(C)=\displaystyle\int_{C\times\mathds{R}_{+}}\mathds{1}_{x\leq\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{m}_{s}}d{P}^{m}(s,x)\\
&\tilde{{W}}^{p}_{k}\leq u_{0}Ke^{-c(k-t_{0})}+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{k-}K_{4}e^{-c(k-s)}d\tilde{{S}}^{m}_{s}\end{aligned}\right..

(D.5)

The searched process is the one which satisfies equality in (
D.5
) and then doing
u_{0}\leftarrow Ku_{0}
concludes.
∎

E
LASSO estimation for HAR processes

The last appendix we present three technical results used in Section
7
. The first one bounds the 1-stationary HAR process over all possible sliding windows of size
A
in the interval
[-A,T)
. The second and third results are respectively, concentration inequalities (generalized Bernstein inequalities) for point processes and gaussian processes.

In our statistical study, considering the support assumption of the interaction functions, we need to control locally the
\bm{{\HAR}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\star}}\bm{(}-\infty\bm{)}
process, denoted
X=(\lambda,S,W)
, on time window of size
A
, over all the time window
[-A,T)
. Informally we have to control
\displaystyle\max_{t\in[0,T)}\|X_{t-A,t}\|_{\infty}
.

Let for
m\in\bm{M}
,

Y^{m}(T)=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{intervals}\ I\subset[-A,T)\\
\text{such that}\ |I|\leq A\end{subarray}}S^{m}(I)

and similarly, for
p\in\bm{P}
and
\alpha\in\bm{M}\cup\bm{P}
,

Y^{\alpha}_{p}(T)=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{intervals}\ I\subset[-A,T)\\
\text{such that}\ |I|\leq A\end{subarray}}\displaystyle\int_{I}|\kappa^{\alpha}_{p}(W^{p}_{k})|d{D}^{p}_{k}.

Finally, let
Y_{T}=\max\big(\max_{m}Y^{m}(T),\ \max_{\alpha,p}Y^{\alpha}_{p}(T)\big)
.

Proposition E.1
.

There exists constants
C^{\star}
and
c^{\star}
depending on
\mathfrak{M}^{\star}
(and thus possibly on
A
) such that for all
T\geq 1
and all
x\geq 0
,

\mathds{P}\left(Y_{T}\leq x\right)\geq 1-C^{\star}Te^{-c^{\star}x}.

Thus for all
q>0
we have,

\mathds{P}\left(Y_{T}\leq C^{\star}(q+1)\log(T)\right)\geq 1-\frac{C^{\star}}{T^{q}}.

If
\mathbb{Y}_{T}
is defined as
Y_{T}
but with
\mathbb{X}
, the coupling from Definition
6.5
, instead of
X
, then the same holds for
\mathbb{Y}_{T}
.

To calibrate the LASSO we need controls on the fluctuations of the contrast around its compensator;
b_{\varphi}-\tilde{b}_{\varphi}
for all
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}
. These fluctuations are broken into two terms:
J_{\varphi,\bm{M}}
and
J_{\varphi,\bm{P}}
. Recall that for
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}
we have

J_{\varphi,\bm{M}}=\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T-}\Psi^{m}(\varphi,t,X_{t-A,t})\big(dS^{m}_{t}-\lambda^{m}_{t}dt\big),

with
\Psi^{m}(\varphi,t,X_{t-A,t})
a predictable process.

Lemma
E.2
is the analogue, for point processes, of Bernstein inequality. It is an adaptation of
[
28
]
and controls the term
J_{\varphi,\bm{M}}
.

Lemma E.2
.

Let
x>0
and
q>0
. If
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{P}}\cup\bm{\Phi}^{\bm{P}}
then we have

\mathds{P}\left(|J_{\varphi,\bm{M}}|=0\right)=1.

Else, define
B_{\varphi}
by

B_{\varphi}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1&&\text{if}\ \varphi\in\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{M}}\\
&C^{\star}(q+1)\log(T)&&\text{if}\ \varphi\in\bm{\Phi}^{\bm{M}}\end{aligned}\right.

and let

V_{\varphi,\bm{M}}=\frac{1}{3-e}\left(B_{\varphi}^{2}x+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\Psi^{m}(\varphi,t,X_{t-A,t})^{2}dS^{m}_{t}\right).

Then we have

|J_{\varphi,\bm{M}}|\leq\sqrt{3xV_{\varphi,\bm{M}}}+\frac{B_{\varphi}x}{3}\leq B_{\varphi}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{3x(x+C^{\star}(q+1)T\log(T))}{3-e}}+\frac{x}{3}\Big)

(E.1)

with probability at least

1-4e^{-x}\frac{\log(1+C^{\star}(q+1)T\log(T)/x)}{\log(3/2)}-\frac{C^{\star}}{T^{q}}.

The second term,
J_{\varphi,\bm{P}}
writes as follow,

J_{\varphi,\bm{P}}=\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T-}\Psi^{p}(\varphi,k,X_{k-A,k})\varepsilon^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}

with
\Psi^{p}(\varphi,k,X_{k-A,k})
a predictable process and
\varepsilon^{p}_{k}
independent and centered gaussian random variables with variance
\sigma_{p}^{2}
.

Lemma
E.3
is a Bernstein like inequality but for generalized gaussian processes. It controls the term
J_{\varphi,\bm{P}}
.

Lemma E.3
.

Let
x>0
and
q>0
. If
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{M}}\cup\bm{\Phi}^{\bm{M}}
then we have

\mathds{P}\left(|J_{\varphi,\bm{P}}|=0\right)=1.

Else, define
B_{\varphi}
by

A_{\varphi}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1&&\text{if}\ \varphi\in\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{P}}\\
&C^{\star}(q+1)\log(T)&&\text{if}\ \varphi\in\bm{\Phi}^{\bm{P}}\end{aligned}\right.

and let

V_{\varphi,\bm{P}}=\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\Psi^{p}(\varphi,k,X_{k-A,k})^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}d{D}^{p}_{k}.

Then there exists
C^{\star}
depending only on
\mathfrak{M}^{\star}
such that, if
\bar{\sigma}=\max_{p\in\bm{P}}\sigma_{p}
, for all
x>0
we have

|J_{\varphi,\bm{P}}|\leq\sqrt{3x(V_{\varphi,\bm{P}}+B_{\varphi}^{2}\bar{\sigma}^{2})}\leq B_{\varphi}\sqrt{C^{\star}xT}

(E.2)

with probability at least

1-2e^{-x}\frac{\log(C^{\star}T)}{\log(3/2)}-\frac{C^{\star}}{T^{q}}.

Remark that
Y_{T}
is an increasing function of
A
so we can suppose that
A\geq 1
. To have only a maximum over a finite number of random variables we can notice that

Y_{T}\leq 2\max_{n=-1,\cdots,\lfloor T/A\rfloor}\max_{m,\alpha,p}\left(S^{m}([nA,nA+A)),\ \displaystyle\int_{nA}^{(nA+A)-}|\kappa^{\alpha}_{p}(W^{p}_{k})|d{D}^{p}_{k}\right)

From the exponential moments we have,

\mathds{P}\left(S^{m}([nA,nA+A))\geq x\right)\leq C^{\star}e^{-c^{\star}x}.

Then, since
|\kappa^{\alpha}_{p}(W^{p}_{k})|\leq C^{\star}(|W^{p}_{k}|+1)
, the exponential moments for HAR processes lead to

\mathds{P}\left(|\kappa^{\alpha}_{p}(W^{p}_{k})|\geq x\right)\leq C^{\star}e^{-c^{\star}x}.

Thus, since
\mathcal{D}_{p}\cap[nA,nA+A)
has less than
(A+1)n_{p}
elements, using the above bound with
x\leftarrow x/(A+1)n_{p}
lead to

\mathds{P}\left(\displaystyle\int_{nA}^{(nA+A)-}|\kappa^{\alpha}_{p}(W^{p}_{k})|d{D}^{p}_{k}\geq x\right)\leq C^{\star}e^{-c^{\star}x}.

Finally, since
\card([\![-1,\lfloor T/A\rfloor]\!]\times(\bm{M}\cup[(\bm{M}\cup\bm{P})\times\bm{P}]))\leq C^{\star}T
it is clear that

\mathds{P}\left(Y_{T}\leq x\right)\geq 1-C^{\star}Te^{-c^{\star}x}.

For the second bound, take
x=\frac{1}{c^{\star}}(q+1)\log(T)
.

Since the proof only uses exponential moments of the process
X=\bm{{\HAR}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\star}}\bm{(}-\infty\bm{)}
, it also holds for the process
\bm{{\HAR}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\star,+}}\bm{(}-\infty\bm{)}
, but since this process dominates the coupling
\mathbb{X}
which consists in several pieces of processes
\bm{{\HAR}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\star}}\bm{(}t_{0}\bm{)}
for
t_{0}\in\mathds{R}
, it is clear that the result also holds for
\mathbb{Y}_{T}
.
∎

We follow the steps of
[
28
]
. First if
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}^{\bm{P}}\cup\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{P}}
it is clear that
J_{\varphi,\bm{M}}=0
so the first part is clear. Suppose now that
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}^{\bm{M}}\cup\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{M}}
. From Theorem 3 of
[
28
]
applied to the process
J_{\varphi,\bm{M}}
with, in the notations of
[
28
]
,
\mu=1
,
\varepsilon=1/2
,
\tau=T
, the following holds.

Proposition E.4
.

Let
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}
. Let
x>0
,
B_{\varphi}>0
, and
v>w>0
constants. Define,

V_{\varphi,\bm{M}}=\frac{1}{3-e}\left(B_{\varphi}^{2}x+\sum_{m\in\bm{M}}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\Psi^{m}(\varphi,t,X_{t-A,t})^{2}dS^{m}_{t}\right).

Then we have,

\displaystyle\mathds{P}\left(|J_{\varphi,\bm{M}}|\geq\sqrt{3V_{\varphi,\bm{M}}x}+\frac{B_{\varphi}x}{3}\ \text{and}\ w\leq V_{\varphi,\bm{M}}\leq v\ \text{and}\ \sup_{m\in\bm{M},t\in[0,T]}|\Psi^{m}(\varphi,t,X_{t-A,t})|\leq B_{\varphi}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4e^{-x}\bigg(\frac{\log(v/w)}{\log(3/2)}+1\bigg).

Let us investigate the choice of
B_{\varphi}
in order to have lower bounds on the probability of the event

\big(\sup_{m\in\bm{M},t\in[0,T]}|\Psi^{m}(\varphi,t,X_{t-A,t})|\leq B_{\varphi}\big).

Suppose that
\varphi
belongs to

1.

\bm{\Phi}_{m}
for
m\in\bm{M}
, then the choice
B_{\varphi}=1
holds with probability
1
.

2.

\bm{\Phi}_{\alpha}^{m}
for
m\in\bm{M}
and
\alpha\in\bm{M}\cup\bm{P}
, then since
\|\bm{\Phi}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, it is clear that
\Psi^{m}(\varphi,t,X_{t-A,t})\leq Y_{T}
for
0\leq t\leq T
. Thus one can take
B_{\varphi}=C^{\star}(q+1)\log(T)
and it holds with probability at least
1-\frac{C^{\star}}{T^{q}}
by Proposition
E.1
.

On the other hand, under the event
\big(\sup_{m\in\bm{M},t\in[0,T]}|\Psi^{m}(\varphi,t,X_{t-A,t})|\leq B_{\varphi}\big)
, we have

\frac{B_{\varphi}^{2}x}{3-e}\leq V_{\varphi,\bm{M}}\leq\frac{B_{\varphi}^{2}(x+S([0,T]))}{3-e}.

From Corollary
5.5
, there exist
\theta_{0}>0
and
K<\infty
such that
\mathds{E}\left[e^{\theta_{0}S([s,s+1])}\right]<K
holds for all
s\in\mathds{R}
. Thus by Markov inequality we have, for any
0\leq t\leq T-1
,

\mathds{P}\left(S([t,t+1])\geq C^{\star}(q+1)\log(T)\right)\leq\frac{C^{\star}}{T^{q+1}},

and thus by union bound,

\mathds{P}\left(S([0,T])\geq C^{\star}(q+1)T\log(T)\right)\leq\frac{C^{\star}}{T^{q}}.

Thus, with probability at least

1-\frac{C^{\star}}{T^{q}}

one can take

w=\frac{B_{\varphi}^{2}x}{3-e},\ \text{and}\ v=\frac{B_{\varphi}^{2}(x+C^{\star}(q+1)T\log(T))}{3-e},\ \text{and}\ B_{\varphi}\ \text{the value discussed above}.

Combining everything together concludes the proof.
∎

First if
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}^{\bm{M}}\cup\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{M}}
it is clear that
J_{\varphi,\bm{M}}=0
so the first part is clear. Suppose now that
\varphi\in\bm{\Phi}^{\bm{P}}\cup\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{P}}
. Recall that the noises
\varepsilon
are independent and distributed as follows,

\varepsilon^{p}_{k}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{p}^{2})

with
\max_{p\in\bm{P}}\sigma_{p}\leq\bar{\sigma}
. The proof is based on Proposition
E.5
which can be found in
[
54
]
Lemma 4. We prove Proposition
E.5
with general notations at the end.

Proposition E.5
.

Let
(H^{p}_{k})_{p\in\bm{P}}
be a predictable process bounded by
B
. Let

M_{t}=\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}H^{p}_{k}\varepsilon^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}

and

V_{t}=\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(H^{p}_{k})^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}d{D}^{p}_{k}.

Then we have for all
x\geq 0
,

\mathds{P}\left(|M_{t}|\geq\sqrt{3x(V_{t}+B^{2}\bar{\sigma}^{2})}\right)\leq 2e^{-x}\Big(1+\frac{\log\big(1+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}(n_{p}t+1)\big)}{\log(3/2)}\Big).

We will apply Proposition
E.5
to

J_{\varphi,\bm{P}}=\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\Psi^{p}(\varphi,k,X_{k-A,k})\varepsilon^{p}_{k}d{D}^{p}_{k}.

Thus we have to choose wisely
B_{\varphi}
to bound with hight probability

\sup_{(p,k)\in\mathcal{Q},0\leq k\leq T}|\Psi^{p}(\varphi,k,X_{k-A,k})|.

Suppose that
\varphi
belongs to

1.

\bm{\Phi}_{p}
for
p\in\bm{P}
, then the choice
B_{\varphi}=1
holds with probability
1
.

2.

\bm{\Phi}_{\alpha}^{p}
for
p\in\bm{P}
and
\alpha\in\bm{M}\cup\bm{P}
, then since
\|\bm{\Phi}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, it is clear that
\Psi^{p}(\varphi,k,X_{k-A,k})\leq Y_{T}
for
0\leq t\leq T
. Thus one can take
B_{\varphi}=C^{\star}(q+1)\log(T)
and it holds with probability at least
1-\frac{C^{\star}}{T^{q}}
by Proposition
E.1
.

This concludes the proof.
∎

By induction one has that

\mathds{E}\left[e^{\xi M_{t}-\frac{\xi^{2}}{2}V_{t}}\right]\leq 1,\quad\xi\geq 0.

Thus for any
x\geq 0
we have

\mathds{P}\left(M_{t}\geq\frac{\xi}{2}V_{t}+\frac{x}{\xi}\right)\leq e^{-x}.

Then we use a peeling argument. Suppose that
0\leq v_{0}\leq V_{t}\leq v_{1}
. Then we have

\mathds{P}\left(M_{t}\geq\frac{\xi}{2}v_{1}+\frac{x}{\xi}\right)\leq e^{-x}.

Then one can choose the optimal
\xi=\sqrt{2x/v_{1}}
and obtains,

\mathds{P}\left(M_{t}\geq\sqrt{2xv_{1}}\right)\leq e^{-x}.

Thus we have

\mathds{P}\left(M_{t}\geq\sqrt{2xv_{1}},\ v_{0}\leq V_{t}\leq v_{1}\right)\leq e^{-x}.

Then if
v_{1}=(1+\varepsilon)v_{0},\cdots,v_{n}=(1+\varepsilon)^{n}v_{0}
we have, by union bound,

\mathds{P}\left(M_{t}\geq\sqrt{2x(1+\varepsilon)V_{t}},\ v_{0}\leq V_{t}\leq v_{n}\right)\leq ne^{-x}.

We want
v_{0}>0
thus we apply this result with
V_{t}+B^{2}\bar{\sigma}\geq B^{2}\bar{\sigma}>0
instead of
V_{t}
. Since
(H^{p}_{k})^{2}\leq B^{2}
it is clear that

V_{t}+B^{2}\bar{\sigma}\leq B^{2}\bar{\sigma}\Big(1+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}(n_{p}t+1)\Big)=:v_{n},

thus we can take
v_{0}=B^{2}\bar{\sigma}
,
\varepsilon=1/2
and
v_{n}
as above. Which leads to

\mathds{P}\left(M_{t}\geq\sqrt{3x(V_{t}+B^{2}\bar{\sigma})}\right)\leq ne^{-x}\leq e^{-x}\Big(1+\frac{\log\big(1+\sum_{p\in\bm{P}}(n_{p}t+1)\big)}{\log(3/2)}\Big).

∎
